(1) R4 now separates the LENT VALUE's mode from the RECEIVER's required access as two facts: an accessor is SHARED-ELIGIBLE iff its shared specialization type-checks as an ordinary &self body under the existing receiver rule, EXCLUSIVE-ONLY otherwise, and the requirement is checked at EVERY call site — a read-only window through an exclusive-only accessor still borrows the receiver exclusively, is refused on a let root naming the mutating line, and two such windows on one var root conflict as two exclusive receiver borrows. The eligibility is recorded on the declaration as a declared annotation field (per instantiation for generics) so imported and generic accessors carry it; no syntactic writes-self scan. (2) R6 lifts #lend_var into &var self accessors and re-keys the design-179 two-specialization split on 'names #lend_var' rather than on the receiver spelling; eligibility is computed on the shared specialization AFTER folding #lend_var=false, so Data.[]'s CoW gate keeps let-reads shared and separation-free (K-f pins it). (3) R7: forwarded lends carry the OUTER window's mode — the shared specialization calls the inner accessor shared (a nested CoW read no longer copies), an outer &var T over an inner &T is refused at the lend, an exclusive-only inner propagates exclusive-only to the outer through the recorded eligibility (K-g pins all three). The forwarded lend is the seventh named entry of the window-opener funnel. Full battery GREEN, 31/31 stages. Awaiting the lead's validation + patch.\n\n- `7117b7d9` SL-333: `borrows` signatures name the lent reference and its mode (compiler R1-R7 + the std/libs/examples corpus + conformance rows K123-K130)\n- `f6fd70d8` SL-333: docs for the `borrows` mode rules (LANGUAGE_SPEC, saw-lang skill, README, todo.md)\n- `dbb61617` SL-333: the specialization twin is a sibling template, not a descendant (two terminal-battery findings, both fixed at the mechanism)\n\n## 1. ONE DIVERGENCE FROM THE RULED BRIEF — the lead should rule\n\nR6 says two things that cannot both hold, and I built the one that makes R7 true as written. **The split key is the DECLARED MODE, not the presence of `#lend_var`.**\n\nR6: \"make the specialization split key on 'the accessor names `#lend_var`' rather than on the receiver spelling\" and, one sentence later, \"An accessor that never names it compiles once, as before.\"\nR7: \"inside the SHARED specialization the inner accessor is invoked shared … inside the EXCLUSIVE one exclusively.\"\n\nA forwarding accessor that never names `#lend_var` has no shared specialization for R7 to speak about, so under the literal key R7 is unimplementable for it — and worse, R4 then makes EVERY such accessor exclusive-only, because its single `&var` lend borrows `self.inner` exclusively and that is a mutation through the receiver like any other. `Outer.[]` in `lend_var_coro_and_forwarding.saw` is exactly that shape and would have stopped working on a `let` root.\n\nWhat I built: a `borrows -> &var T` accessor compiles as two specializations (it is precisely the declaration a use site may open either way), a `borrows -> &T` one compiles once. `#lend_var` keeps its meaning unchanged — it NAMES the specialization being compiled — and the always-constant rule survives, re-keyed with the split: `#lend_var` in a `-> &T` accessor is always false and is the same error design 179 gave for the receiver spelling it used to key on (`errors/lend_var_in_shared_lend_accessor.saw`, the repurposed `lend_var_in_var_self_accessor.saw`).\n\nCost, measured: the shared copy of a `-> &var T` accessor is a second body checked and emitted. Nothing in the final binary for an accessor used one way only (unreferenced internal functions are DCE'd); the `bench` lane's checksums and timings are unchanged and the whole battery is green. If the lead prefers the literal key, R7's K129 cell 1 and the `lend_var_coro_and_forwarding` outcome flip back, and the report's OPEN moves there.\n\nTwo derived rules I wrote because the ruling's mechanism required them, both flagged:\n- `#lend_var` in a `borrows -> &T` accessor is an error (the design-179 rule re-keyed off the receiver onto the mode, same reason: an always-constant gate reads as a live decision).\n- R2 is checked after the receiver validation, so `&self ... borrows -> &var T` gets the mode error and a by-value `self` still gets the receiver error first.\n\n## 2. The window-opener funnel: seven entries × three outcomes\n\n`place_uses._window_call` is the one chokepoint; its docstring now names its entries as an author spells them, and both declaration facts (R1's mode, R4's eligibility) are read there and nowhere else. Every cell below has compile/run evidence — the permitted column from `examples/conformance/K123_every_window_opener_reads_the_declared_mode.saw` (runs, output asserted), the refused column from `examples/errors/shared_lend_write_refused_at_every_opener.saw` (six diagnostics in one compile) plus the two R7 error pins.\n\n| # | entry (author's spelling) | internal caller | `-> &T` + write | `-> &var T` shared | `-> &var T` exclusive |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | subscript read/chain `b[i]`, `b[i].n` | `_chain_window` | n/a (no write) | ok | — |\n| 2 | named accessor `b.at(i)`, `b.at(i).n` | `_chain_window` | n/a (no write) | ok | — |\n| 3 | write through either, `b[i] = x`, `b[i].n += 1` | `_assignment` | REFUSED (`v[…]`, `v.at(…)`) | — | ok |\n| 4 | `?.` chain assignment `b.pair(i)?.n = v` | `_chain_assign_window` | REFUSED (`v.pair(…)`) | — | ok |\n| 5 | `!` head, `b.get(i)!`, `b.pair(i)!.n = v` | `_chain_window` / `_assignment` | REFUSED (`v.pair(…)`) | ok | ok |\n| 6 | `match` / `if let` on a place (DF-146d payload lend) | `_borrow_match`, `_presence_condition` | n/a — the opener is shared by construction; the read compiles on both modes | ok | — |\n| 7 | reference argument `f(&b[i])` / `f(&var b[i])` | `_span_call` | REFUSED (`v[…]`) | ok | ok |\n| 8 | forwarded `lend self.inner[i]` inside another accessor | `_span_call` (from_lend) | REFUSED at the lend (`self.inner[…]`, R7 cell 2) | ok (R7 cell 1 — no inner gate) | ok |\n\n(Seven openers in the funnel, eight rows: the write and the `&var` argument are two spellings of one entry and two different things to a reader.)\n\nEach refusal names the accessor, its declared mode and the fixit, verbatim:\n``cannot write through `v[…]`: `View.[]` is declared `borrows -> &Int`, which lends the place READ-ONLY — a use site may narrow a declared window mode, never widen it`` / hint ``declare the accessor `borrows -> &var Int` to let a use site write through the place it lends (its receiver must then be `&var self`), or reach the storage through a `&var self` method of `View```.\n\n## 3. R4 eligibility per std accessor\n\nComputed by the receiver-permission funnel itself: `_self_borrow_is_exclusive`'s five named entry points now RECORD what they find as well as refuse it, onto `Method.place_receiver_mutation` (a DECLARED `annotation(...)` field — the `astgraft` lane is green), on the SHARED specialization, closed over the forwarding graph in `place_uses.propagate_forwarded_eligibility` before anything is lowered, and read at every call site by `_window_call` alone. The `lend`'s own reference is exempt by `from_lend` — that is the place the accessor exists to hand out.\n\n| accessor | declared | shared-eligible | why |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `Vector.[]` | `&var self -> &var T` | yes | body is a bounds check + `lend buf[index]` |\n| `Vector.get` | `&var self -> &var T?` | yes | same, conditional |\n| `Map.[]` | `&var self -> &var V?` | yes | `_find` is `&self`; the arm lends the payload |\n| `Map.get` | `&var self -> &var V?` | yes | same |\n| `Map._key_ref` | `&self -> &K?` | yes | `&self`, trivially |\n| `Map._value_ref` | `&self -> &V?` | yes | `&self`, trivially |\n| `Data.[]` | `&var self -> &var UInt8` | yes | the CoW gate (`self._make_ready`, a `&var self` call on `self`) folds OUT of the shared specialization — this is the R6 order the whole rule turns on |\n| `Box.value` | `&var self -> &var T` | yes | `lend self.ptr[0]` |\n| `JsonValue.as_array` | `&var self -> &var Vector<JsonValue>?` | yes | match arm payload lend |\n| `JsonValue.as_object` | `&var self -> &var Map<String, JsonValue>?` | yes | same |\n| `Slot.value` | `&var self -> &var T` | yes | a guard + `lend self.v!` |\n| `UnsafeRef.deref` | `&var self -> &var T` | yes | `lend self.p[0]` |\n| `TomlSection.table` | `&self -> &TomlTable?` | yes | `&self`, trivially |\n| `TomlDoc.section_at` | `&self -> &TomlSection` | yes | `&self`, trivially |\n| `TomlDoc.section` | `&self -> &TomlSection?` | yes | `&self`, trivially |\n\nEvery one of the fifteen is shared-eligible, so R2's move to `&var self` costs no caller a `let` root. Direct evidence: a probe that reads through `Vector.[]`, `Vector.get`, `Map.[]`, `Map.get`, `Data.[]`, `Box.value`, `JsonValue.as_object` and `Slot.value` on `let` bindings compiles and runs. The corpus's own exclusive-only examples are hand-written (`Bag.tally`, `Ledger.entry`, `Inner.[]` with an ungated gate).\n\n## 4. R6 — the `Data.[]` proof\n\n`examples/conformance/K128_cow_gate_survives_the_var_self_spelling.saw`, run output:\n\n```\nlet read 10, gates 0          # hand-written gate, `let` root: the gate does not run\nvar read 10, gates 0\nvar write 11, gates 1          # …and does on a write\nData: frozen read 7, no var needed\nData: after buf[0] = 90 -> frozen 7, buf 90     # the partner keeps its bytes\nData: two readers of one buffer see 7 and 7\n```\n\n`Data.[]` is now `&var self ... borrows -> &var UInt8` with the CoW gate under `#lend_var`. The shared specialization folds the constant false, the gate is REMOVED, R4 is computed on that body, and `let frozen = load(); print(frozen[0])` needs no `var` and separates nothing. `examples/data_place_shared_read.saw` and the `lend_var_*.saw` set keep their outcomes unchanged (K130's control row).\n\n## 5. R7 — three cells\n\n- **cell 1, shared forwarding costs nothing.** `K129_forwarded_lend_carries_the_window_mode.saw`: `shared forward reads 3, inner gates 0` / `exclusive forward writes 4, inner gates 1` / `shared forward reads again 4, inner gates 1`, and a nested `Data` read through a forwarding accessor leaves the sharing partner's bytes alone. `lend_var_coro_and_forwarding.saw` — design 179's own statement of the limit — CHANGED OUTCOME to `inner gates 0` / `inner gates 1` (was 1 / 3); its header now states R7 instead of the limit.\n- **cell 2, no upgrade.** `errors/forwarded_lend_cannot_upgrade_an_inner_shared_lend.saw`: an outer `-> &var Int` over an inner `-> &Int` is R1's refusal reaching entry 8 — ``cannot write through `self.inner[…]`: `Inner.[]` is declared `borrows -> &Int`…``, anchored at the `lend`.\n- **cell 3, exclusive-only propagates.** `errors/forwarded_lend_inherits_exclusive_only.saw`: ``cannot read through the place window `[]` opens on immutable variable `frozen`: … — the forwarded lend through `Inner.[]` at line 39``. Propagated through the RECORDED bit, as a fixpoint over the forwarding graph, so an imported or generic forwarder carries it.\n\n## 6. The corpus rewrite\n\n105 pre-existing declarations, all rewritten; 30 new ones in the K-row files.\n\n| tree | declarations | `-> &var T` | `-> &T` |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `sawc/std` | 12 (6 files) | 10 | 2 (`Map._key_ref`, `Map._value_ref`) |\n| `libs/toml` | 3 (1 file) | 0 | 3 |\n| `examples` | 90 (61 files) | 57 | 33 |\n| (new K-row files) | 30 (12 files) | 19 | 9 + 2 deliberately malformed |\n| `blade`, `devtools`, `tools` | 0 | — | — |\n\nOracles, as the brief set them:\n- **std + libs by the API's intent.** Subscript / `get` / view-shaped: `Vector.[]`/`get`, `Map.[]`/`get`, `Data.[]`, `Box.value`, `Slot.value`, `UnsafeRef.deref`, `JsonValue.as_array`/`as_object` are `&var self -> &var T` (the spec's own worked examples write through every one, and `doc.as_object()!.insert(…)` in four corpus files is an exclusive use of `as_object`). Views that must not be written through: `Map._key_ref` (a key write would lose the entry in its own table) and `Map._value_ref` (both are read-only probe paths, used only as `…!.copy()`), and all three `libs/toml` accessors (every use in `libs/toml/tests`, `blade/src/manifest.saw` and the spec is a read; the docstrings say \"reached IN the document rather than handed out\").\n- **examples from their use sites,** decided by the COMPILER rather than by reading: phase A set every declaration to the permissive `&var self -> &var T`, phase B then tried demoting each one to `&self -> &T` and kept the demotion only when the file's OUTCOME was byte-identical (same exit status, same first diagnostic). 35 demotions, 51 kept. Five demotions were reverted by hand, each for a reason the per-file oracle cannot see: `examples/modules/placecell.saw`'s `at`/`maybe` (a MODULE, whose only writer is the consumer `place_cross_module_same_name_type.saw` — caught by the full suite), `doc_emit_borrows.saw`'s `[]`/`get` and `R33`'s `writable` (files whose purpose is to exhibit both modes).\n\n**NO OPEN CELLS.** Every accessor's intent was settled by a docstring, a use site, or a compile.\n\nChanged outcomes, listed:\n1. `examples/lend_var_coro_and_forwarding.saw` — gate counts, by R7 (§5).\n2. `examples/errors/lend_var_ungated_receiver_mutation.saw` — R2 makes the receiver mutation legal at the declaration and R4 moves the refusal to the use site, naming the mutating line. Header rewritten, two EXPECT lines.\n3. `examples/errors/lend_var_in_var_self_accessor.saw` → renamed `lend_var_in_shared_lend_accessor.saw` — R6 deletes the rule it pinned and re-keys it onto the mode; the file pins the re-keyed rule.\n4. `examples/conformance/R33_borrows_lends_ref_type.saw` — R5 INVERTS its guarantee (`borrows -> &Int` is now the correct spelling). Rewritten as R5's own row: the `borrows` forms are accepted beside the plain method that is still refused; INDEX row text updated.\n5. `examples/errors/ref_return_method.saw` — the fixit text now names `-> &var Int`.\n6. `examples/errors/shared_self_borrows_epilogue_write.saw` and `…_field_var_method_call.saw` — kept their original rule by staying `&self ... borrows -> &Int` (the declaration the rule is about), with a note pointing at the R4 pin for the `&var self` spelling.\n\nNothing else in the corpus changed outcome, and **no test was worked around**: every site that stopped compiling is a classification decision above.\n\n## 7. Obligation 2 — the consumer sweep\n\n- `bootstrap` lane green with **zero changes to `blade/` or `libs/*/tests`**: stage0→stage2, 22 blade tests, toml 4, semver 4, imgformat 2. Blade is the real Saw consumer of `Map`, `Vector`, `Data`, `JsonValue` and `libs/toml`, and it needed no edit at all.\n- **sawtracker (`/Users/shawn/Projects/sawtracker/src/main.saw`, read-only)** — stop point IDENTICAL before and after. Both compilers stop at `src/main.saw:197:35` with ``cannot suspend in a `Thread.spawn { ... }` body: closure calls `run_test_job` → `execute_tests` → `absolute` → `checked` → `command` → `Command.output` → io_wait``. That is a pre-existing defect unrelated to this brief; SL-333 does not move it in either direction. Nothing in that repo was edited.\n\n## 8. Conformance rows (obligation 3)\n\nK123-K130, INDEX header recounted to **551 rows: 288 carry a file here, 263 covered elsewhere** (was 543/284/259).\n\n- K123 every window opener reads the declared mode — `K123_every_window_opener_reads_the_declared_mode.saw` + `errors/shared_lend_write_refused_at_every_opener.saw` (the §2 matrix, both halves)\n- K124 the receiver mode bounds the return mode — `errors/borrows_var_lend_needs_var_self.saw`\n- K125 receiver access is a second fact — `K125_receiver_access_is_a_second_fact.saw` + `errors/exclusive_only_accessor_on_let_root.saw` + `errors/two_exclusive_only_windows_conflict.saw`\n- K126 the bare `borrows -> T` is an error with a fixit — `errors/borrows_bare_return_type.saw`\n- K127 the conditional lend carries a mode too — `K127_conditional_lend_modes.saw`\n- K128 `#lend_var` in a `&var self -> &var T` accessor, gate free for a read — `K128_cow_gate_survives_the_var_self_spelling.saw`\n- K129 a forwarded lend carries the window mode — `K129_forwarded_lend_carries_the_window_mode.saw` + the two R7 error pins\n- K130 CONTROL: the design-141/146/188 window rules unchanged in outcome — eight existing `examples/` files\n\n## 9. Obligation 4 — the two terminal-battery findings\n\nBoth were caused by this change reaching ground the old split never touched, and both are fixed at the MECHANISM, not the symptom (commit `dbb61617`):\n\n1. **`transferdecisions`** (design 270's preservation audit, P3). `ASTNode.__deepcopy__` stamps `origin_node_id` because its other two callers produce SPECIALIZATIONS, and the audit hops back along that stamp to check that a clone RESOLVED what its template deferred. The `#lend_var` twin resolves nothing — it is the same generic body compiled for the other flavor. Invisible while only `Data.[]` had a twin (not generic, no abstract deferral to inherit); re-keying the split gave `Map.[]`/`Map.get` twins and every mono instance of one read as a specialization that had inherited its template's deferral. The twin now clears the stamp, so its own instances hop back to IT. The mechanism's siblings were enumerated: `__deepcopy__`'s other callers are monomorphization and trait-default synthesis, both genuine specializations, both correct to stamp.\n2. **`docverify`** — the two new `saw-error` blocks in the spec's Places section are compiled as-is, so they are whole programs now.\n\nNo compiler defect outside the unit was hit, so **no new SL issue was filed**.\n\n## 10. Gates\n\n- Per-commit, under the machine-wide suite lock: full suite + `tools/freestanding_runner.py` (both arches). Suite **2589 passed, 10 xfailed, 0 failed** (up from 2577/10 on main — 12 new tests). Freestanding **36 passed across riscv32 + arm64**.\n- Terminal: `tools/battery.sh` full, **31 of 31 stages GREEN in 3618s**, including `irdet --all` (918s, whole corpus), `reemit` (728s), `bootstrap` (328s), `corodiff`, `astgraft`, `transferdecisions`, `docverify`, `corototality`, `corodiscovery`, `fuzz`, `gmgate`, `freestanding`. Exit code 0.\n- The xfail set is unchanged (10, all pre-existing and cited). R4 says the exclusive-only accessor borrows its receiver EXCLUSIVELY at every use \"exactly as two `&var self` calls would\", and the control P10b shows a shared window beside an exclusive one on one root is refused. The two-exclusive-only pin (`errors/two_exclusive_only_windows_conflict.saw`) passes, which says the check is a special case for two exclusive-only windows rather than the receiver borrow's mode feeding the ordinary pairwise root-charge test. Keying the split on the DECLARED MODE (`-> &var T` compiles twice, `-> &T` once) is the reading that makes R4/R6/R7 consistent; the derived rule \"`#lend_var` in a `-> &T` accessor is an error\" is design 179's always-constant rule re-keyed. Measured cost: a second body per writable accessor, DCE'd when unused; bench unchanged. The R7 forwarding split and R4's recorded eligibility bit are unaffected by it.\n","created":"1790008816","id":"c4"},{"author":"agent:claude-sl333","body":"ADDENDUM (agent:claude-sl333, Sep 21) — the R4 gap is closed at the mechanism. Branch `sl333` tip `aff3d4c4`, full battery 31/31 GREEN. Still not submitted as a patch.\n\n`aff3d4c4` SL-333: the receiver borrow's MODE is what the root charge compares\n\n## The mechanism\n\nIt WAS a special case, exactly as diagnosed: a bespoke scan (`place_uses._reject_nested_exclusive_receiver`) over the nested window lowering, which could only ever see TWO exclusive-only windows. Deleted.\n\nThe receiver borrow's mode is now ONE fact — `typechecker/types.py::_reference_access_mode`, \"the mode a by-reference argument borrows its ROOT at: the sigil, or exclusive when the accessor behind the place is exclusive-only\" — and it is the mode design 188's ordinary pairwise root-charge test reads when it builds its access entries, for top-level and nested reference arguments alike. Every pairing falls out of that one test; there is no second rule left.\n\nWHERE IT IS SETTLED, and why not at the call. A module type-checks its free functions BEFORE its extension methods, so a use site is routinely reached before the accessor whose BODY decides whether it requires an exclusive receiver — asking at the call would answer \"shared\" by accident of ordering. So a call whose access set still holds an unsettled place entry is kept, and those pairs are settled at END OF MODULE (`_settle_place_access_modes`), after every body has been checked. The forwarded-lend closure moved out of `place_uses` into the checker (`propagate_forwarded_place_eligibility`) and runs immediately before it, for the same reason and in that order. Only pairs BOTH sides of which read 'imm' at the call are looked at, so nothing is reported twice and no existing diagnostic moved.\n\n## The sibling matrix, compile evidence, before → after\n\nFixture: one `var` root, `Bag.[]` SHARED-ELIGIBLE, `Bag.tally` EXCLUSIVE-ONLY (epilogue write), `Bag.opt` exclusive-only and CONDITIONAL, `Wrap.fwd` exclusive-only by FORWARDING.\n\n| # | cell | before | after |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| a | `pair(&b[0], &b.tally(1))` — shared-eligible first | COMPILES, prints 30 | REFUSED |\n| b | `pair(&b.tally(0), &b[1])` — exclusive-only first | COMPILES, prints 30 | REFUSED |\n| c | `pair(&b.tally(0), &b.tally(1))` — both exclusive-only | refused (special case) | REFUSED (ordinary test) |\n| e | `pair_cell(&b[0], &b.opt(1)!)` — through a `!` head | COMPILES, prints 12 | REFUSED |\n| g | `with_root(&b.tally(0), &b)` — beside a plain `&root` | COMPILES, prints 10 | REFUSED |\n| h | `with_var_root(&b.tally(0), &var b)` — beside `&var root` | refused (sigil half) | REFUSED |\n| i | `with_var_root(&b[0], &var b)` — shared-eligible beside `&var root` | refused (sigil half) | REFUSED |\n| j | `pair(&w.fwd(0), &w.fwd2(1))` — both exclusive-only by FORWARDING | refused | REFUSED |\n| f | `pair(&b[0], &b[1])` — both shared-eligible (CONTROL) | compiles, 30 | COMPILES, 30 |\n\nThe lead's three probes, re-run on the tip: `p10_mixed_windows_conflict` and `p10c_exclusive_only_first` both refused, each naming `Bag.tally`, the line that mutates `self`, and saying the window only reads; `p10b_control_shared_beside_var` keeps design 188's own wording unchanged.\n\n## SL-334 — a PRE-EXISTING hole the sweep turned up, fixed in the same walk\n\nCell e was not R4's. `_build_access_path` — the walk that builds design 188's access entries — stepped through a `?.` hop and NOT through a `!`, so a `!`-headed place fell out to \"not a path\" and **never joined a call's access set at all**. On `main`, on the design-141 spelling, `setboth(&var b.opt(0)!, &var b.opt(1)!)` compiles with both writes landing, and so does a window beside a `&var` of its own root — X16's and X31's own shapes, one spelling over. Filed as **SL-334** with the repro; fixed here because it is one clause of the same walk (`ForceUnwrap` joins the `BindOptional`/`OptionalEvalExpr` clause; `_render_lvalue_path` renders `x!` to match, which is why two diagnostics stopped saying `<expr>`). The lead may close it at the landing or split the clause out.\n\n## Pins and rows\n\n- `examples/errors/mixed_window_modes_conflict.saw` — the matrix, four cells in one compile (both orders, the `!` head, the plain `&root`), each naming the accessor, its mutating line and \"this window only reads\".\n- `examples/errors/place_window_through_a_forced_lend_charges_its_root.saw` — SL-334, both of its shapes.\n- `examples/errors/two_exclusive_only_windows_conflict.saw` — re-pinned on the design-188 diagnostic it now shares with the rest; its header says there is no separate rule.\n- INDEX: K125 gains the mixed pin and the \"no second rule\" paragraph; **X41** is new (the `!`-head root charge). Header recounted to **552 rows: 288 here, 264 elsewhere**.\n- LANGUAGE_SPEC: R4's paragraph now says a read-only result conflicts with ANY other by-reference access of the root and points at the root charge, with a compiled `saw-error` example; the root-charge paragraph says the mode may come from the accessor and that `v.get(i)!` charges `v` exactly as `v[i]` does.\n\n## Gates\n\n- Per-commit under the lock: suite **2591 passed, 10 xfailed, 0 failed**; freestanding **36 passed across riscv32 + arm64**.\n- Terminal on the tip: `tools/battery.sh` full, **31 of 31 GREEN in 3342s** (irdet --all 942s, reemit 746s, bootstrap 338s, docverify, transferdecisions, astgraft, corodiff, fuzz, gmgate, freestanding). Exit code 0.\n- Corpus cost of the whole commit: ONE existing pin's EXPECT text (`two_exclusive_only_windows_conflict`, now the shared diagnostic). The widened access set from SL-334 changed no other outcome anywhere in the corpus, blade and libs included.\n","created":"1790010374","id":"c5"},{"author":"agent:claude-sawlang","body":"Submitted patch SL-333.p1 (revision 1): SL-333: borrows signatures name the lent reference and its mode — borrows -> &T / -> &var T (+ ? twins), receiver bounds return mode, use site narrows, R4 receiver eligibility recorded per declaration and fed to the root charge (mixed cell fixed at the mechanism), R6 split keyed on the DECLARED MODE (amended r3, user-ruled), R7 forwarded lends carry the outer mode; 105 declarations rewritten (std 12 / libs 3 / examples 90), K123-K130 + X41; fixes SL-334 (a !-headed place never joined the root charge); battery 31/31","created":"1790010449","id":"c6"}],"created":"1789929966","description":"BRIEF (RULED Sep 20 2026, user; lead-written, lives in this issue rather than designs/; r2 folds codex's review, c1; r3 (Sep 21, user ruling on c2 §1 / c3) AMENDS R6's split key — the specialization split is keyed on the DECLARED MODE, and the constant's retirement is filed as SL-335 for after U3): `borrows` signatures name the lent reference and its mode — `borrows -> &T` / `-> &var T` (and the `?` twins), `&self` / `&var self` bounding it. QUEUED after design 275 U2 (SL-318.p6) lands and BEFORE U3 dispatches, because U3 writes the borrowing-struct spec text and its K rows against whatever spelling is current.\n\n## 0. Why\n\nTwo things, one earned by design 275 U3 and one older.\n(a) After U3, `borrows -> X` means two different things: design 141's \"lends a PLACE of X\" and U3's \"returns a VALUE of borrowing-struct type X that carries a borrow\". Same signature shape, disambiguated only by looking up whether X is declared `borrows struct` somewhere else — the reader-visibility doctrine's exact complaint. With `borrows -> &T` for a lend the signature says which: a `&T` result IS the place, a struct result is a value carrying one. And the rule unifies with the field rule U3 ruled: a reference type may appear in a RETURN position only under a `borrows` signature, and in a FIELD only under a `borrows struct` — one sentence, one check.\n(b) Design 141 made every lend writable at the use site's discretion (\"one `&self` declaration, the use site picks shared vs exclusive\"). An accessor AUTHOR therefore cannot express a read-only lend at all. The user's ruling reverses the default: the DECLARATION states the maximum mode, the use site narrows within it.\n\n## 1. The rules (RULED; R4/R6/R7 tightened per codex c1; R6 amended r3)\n\nR1. RETURN TYPE = the WINDOW's MAXIMUM mode. `borrows -> &var T` may open shared or exclusive windows; `borrows -> &T` opens shared only, and a write through it (`g.at(4).weight += 1`, `v[i] = x` through a `&T` subscript accessor, `&var g.at(4)` as an argument) is a compile error naming the accessor and its declared mode, with a fixit naming `-> &var T`. Conditional lends: `-> &var T?` / `-> &T?`. The bare `borrows -> T` spelling on a lend-shaped body is an ERROR with a fixit naming `-> &T` — no silent acceptance of both spellings (no-degradation doctrine). `borrows -> S` where `S` is a `borrows struct` stays the U3 value-returning form (this brief does not build it; it only leaves the spelling free).\nR2. RECEIVER MODE BOUNDS RETURN MODE. `-> &var T` REQUIRES `&var self`: an exclusive place cannot be projected from a shared receiver (design 106's no-upgrade rule one position over). `&var self borrows -> &T` is legal (an accessor that mutates its own bookkeeping but lends read-only). `&self borrows -> &var T` is the teaching error, fixit naming `&var self`.\nR3. THE USE SITE STILL NARROWS the WINDOW. A read through a `&var T` lend opens a SHARED window; two shared windows on one root compose; a write opens the exclusive one. Unchanged from design 141 for every use site within the declared maximum.\nR4. THE LENT VALUE's MODE and the RECEIVER's REQUIRED ACCESS are TWO facts (codex point 1). The window mode is R1/R3. The receiver access an accessor REQUIRES at a call is a property of its BODY: SHARED-ELIGIBLE if the shared specialization of the body (R6) type-checks as an ordinary `&self` body under the existing receiver rule (`typechecker/statements.py`'s \"a `&var self` method / a write reached through a `&self` receiver\" check — no mutation of `self` outside the lent place survives the fold); EXCLUSIVE-ONLY otherwise (a prologue counter, an epilogue update, a `self.x = …` beside the `lend`, a `&var self` method call on self — anything the fold does not remove). This is CHECKED AT EVERY CALL SITE, not only on `let` receivers: a shared use of a shared-eligible `&var self` accessor borrows the receiver SHARED (so it works on a `let` root and two such windows compose); a use of an exclusive-only accessor borrows the receiver EXCLUSIVELY even when the WINDOW it opens is read-only — so it is refused on a `let` root naming the mutating line, and TWO concurrent read-only windows through an exclusive-only accessor on one `var` root CONFLICT (two exclusive receiver borrows), exactly as two `&var self` calls would — and so does an exclusive-only window beside a shared-eligible one, or beside a plain `&`/`&var` of the root (lead validation c3: the receiver borrow's mode is the ONE fact the pairwise root-charge test compares, for every pair, never a special case for two exclusive-only windows). Two read-only results never imply two compatible receiver borrows. The eligibility is RECORDED on the declaration as a DECLARED annotation field (design 126 contract, `astgraft` lane), computed once from the specialized body, so IMPORTED and GENERIC accessors (per instantiation, since the fold's outcome can depend on the instantiation) carry it to their callers — never re-derived by a syntactic \"writes self\" scan at the use site. Ordinary (non-`borrows`) `&var self` methods are UNCHANGED: on a `let` receiver they stay an error.\nR5. ONE RULE FOR REFERENCE TYPES OUTSIDE PARAMETERS. `&T`/`&var T` is legal as a return type iff the function is `borrows` (it is the lend); as a struct field iff the struct is `borrows struct` (U3 builds that half; this brief adds the return half and writes the sentence once in the spec so U3 only adds its clause). Everywhere else a reference type in a non-parameter position keeps today's error.\nR6 (AMENDED r3). THE SPECIALIZATION SPLIT IS KEYED ON THE DECLARED MODE, and `#lend_var` IS LIFTED INTO `&var self` ACCESSORS (codex point 2). Today the constant is forbidden in a `&var self`-declared accessor because that spelling was exclusive-only at every use. Under R1-R4 the writable accessors — `Vector.[]`, `Data.[]` (the copy-on-write gate lives under `#lend_var`), every `&var self -> &var T` — move to that spelling and compile as TWO specializations through the EXISTING design-179 machinery. The key is the DECLARED MODE: every `borrows -> &var T` accessor compiles twice — it is precisely the declaration a use site may open either way, and R7 needs the shared body to EXIST even when the constant is never named (a forwarding accessor's inner call follows the outer window's mode; under r2's key \"names `#lend_var`\" a plain forwarder had no shared body, and R4 would have read its single `&var` lend of `self.inner` as a receiver mutation, making every forwarder exclusive-only) — and a `borrows -> &T` accessor compiles once. The shared specialization folds `#lend_var = false` and REMOVES what it gated, the exclusive one folds `true`. R4's eligibility is computed on the SHARED specialization AFTER the fold, so an exclusive-only CoW branch (`if #lend_var { self._make_ready(...) }`) does not make ordinary reads exclusive: `let frozen = load(); print(frozen[0])` keeps working with no separation and no `var`. The rule \"`#lend_var` is a compile error in a `&var self` accessor\" is DELETED from the spec and RE-KEYED onto the mode: naming `#lend_var` in a `borrows -> &T` accessor is the error (always false — one specialization, the constant decides nothing; design 179's always-constant rule, same reason); the rule \"outside a `borrows` body it is an error\" stays. NO new syntactic scan: eligibility = \"does the specialized shared body pass the ordinary permission rules\". The constant's RETIREMENT in favour of one declaration per lend mode is SL-335, sequenced after U3 — not this brief.\nR7. FORWARDED LENDS carry the OUTER window's mode (codex point 3). Today `lend other[i]` reaches the inner accessor EXCLUSIVELY whichever specialization runs, because `lend X` hands `X` over as `&var X` (spec: \"a shared read of a nested copy-on-write buffer will copy\"). Under R1-R3 the inner call is made with the OUTER window's mode: inside the SHARED specialization the inner accessor is invoked shared (its `&T` window, its shared specialization, no CoW separation), inside the EXCLUSIVE one exclusively. Two refusals fall out and are rows: an outer `-> &var T` accessor whose lend is backed by an inner `-> &T` accessor is an error at the outer `lend` (no upgrade — the inner cannot supply an exclusive place; fixit: declare the inner `-> &var T` or lend your own storage); an outer `-> &T` accessor forwarding through an inner `-> &var T` accessor never upgrades it (the inner is called shared, and the inner's R4 receiver requirement applies to the forwarded receiver: an exclusive-only inner accessor makes the outer exclusive-only too, propagated through the recorded eligibility). The spec sentence about the nested CoW copy is replaced by the new rule.\n\n## 2. The unit (one Opus agent; the corpus rewrite is mechanical and rides inside)\n\nCompiler: parser accepts a reference type after `borrows ->` (and `?`-suffixed); typechecker: (i) the lend-coverage check (design 141) also checks the lent place's mode against the declared return mode and the receiver mode (R1, R2); (ii) the use-site window opener reads the declared maximum and refuses a write through `&T` (R1) — ONE funnel (obligation 1): the place-window opener whose docstring names its entries (subscript `v[i]`, named accessor call, `?.` head, `!` head, `match`/`if let` payload lend per DF-146d, reference-argument `&v[i]`/`&var v[i]`, and the FORWARDED `lend X` inside another accessor — R7's entry); (iii) R4's receiver-eligibility bit, computed ONCE per accessor (per instantiation for generics) from the shared specialization via the existing receiver rule, recorded as a DECLARED annotation field, read at every call site by the same funnel to pick the receiver borrow's mode; (iv) R6: delete the `&var self` fence on `#lend_var`, make the specialization split key on the DECLARED RETURN MODE (r3) rather than on the receiver spelling; (v) R7: the forwarded-lend lowering passes the outer mode; (vi) the old bare spelling, the `&self -> &var T` combination, `#lend_var` in a `-> &T` accessor, and the R7 upgrade each get their fixit error. `--emit-docs` carries the return mode and the receiver eligibility.\nCorpus rewrite: 107 `borrows ->` declarations (Sep-20 census: sawc/std 6 files, examples 61, libs 1; blade/devtools/sawtracker none). Per-site oracle, no guessing: std + libs by the API's intent (a subscript / `get` / `with_*`-shaped accessor is `&var self -> &var T`; a view that must not be written through is `&self -> &T`; when the intent is not obvious from the docstring + existing uses, flag OPEN in the report rather than pick); examples from their use sites (any exclusive use anywhere → `&var`; otherwise `&`), each flip noted in the file's header comment where the file is a pin/row. Every existing test keeps its outcome unless the brief's rule changes it, and each changed outcome is listed. The `#lend_var` examples (`lend_var_*.saw`, `data_place_shared_read.saw`) are R6's regression set and must keep their outcomes; `lend_var_coro_and_forwarding.saw` is R7's, and its nested-CoW expectation changes if it pinned the copy.\nDocs (design 125): LANGUAGE_SPEC \"Places (`borrows` and `lend`)\" — the signature grammar, R1-R7, a new \"read-only lends\" paragraph, the `#lend_var` section's third rule deleted and the two-specializations paragraph re-keyed on the mode, the forwarded-lend sentence replaced, the conditional-lend spellings, the 14 mentions; saw-lang skill (7 mentions + the cheat-sheet line); README if it shows a `borrows` signature.\n\n## 3. Conformance rows FIRST (obligation 3 — a new guarantee: \"a shared lend cannot be written through\", and R4's \"receiver access is never widened by a read-only result\")\n\nK rows continue after U2's last number; the header count stays honest. Rows: (K-a) a write through a `-> &T` lend is refused at the use site, all seven entries of the window opener (matrix, one cell per entry incl. the forwarded lend); (K-b) `&self -> &var T` is refused at the declaration; (K-c) R4 both ways: a shared-eligible `&var self -> &var T` accessor used shared on a `let` receiver works and two such windows compose; an exclusive-only accessor (bookkeeping in the epilogue) is refused on a `let` receiver naming the line, AND two concurrent read-only windows through it on a `var` root are refused as two exclusive receiver borrows, AND (r3, c3) an exclusive-only window beside a shared-eligible one on the same root is refused in both orders; (K-d) the bare `borrows -> T` spelling is refused with the fixit; (K-e) `-> &var T?` / `-> &T?` conditional lends — present/absent × shared/exclusive; (K-f) R6: `Data.[]` declared `&var self -> &var UInt8` with its CoW gate under `#lend_var` — a read on a `let` binding does not separate and needs no `var`, a write separates, the sharing partner keeps its bytes; (K-g) R7: forwarding through the shared specialization calls the inner accessor shared (a nested CoW read does NOT copy — measured by the partner's bytes/refcount), an outer `&var T` over an inner `&T` is refused at the lend, an exclusive-only inner makes the outer exclusive-only; (K-h) control: existing window rows (LIFO epilogues, root charge, `v.push` inside a window) unchanged in outcome. Obligation 2 sweep (a behavioural-contract flip: use sites that WRITE through an accessor the rewrite classifies `&T` stop compiling; accessors whose body mutates self become exclusive-only at every call): the corpus rewrite IS the sweep — every site that stops compiling is a classification decision listed in the report, never a workaround.\n\n## 4. Gates + conduct\n\nPer-commit: full suite + freestanding (both arches) under the suite lock as CLAUDE.md prescribes; terminal: full battery incl. `irdet --all`, `reemit`, `bootstrap` (libs/blade compile under the new spelling), `corodiff`. Linear branch, commits: (1) K rows; (2) parser + typechecker + fixits (R1-R5), (3) R6 + R7 (the specialization re-key and the forwarded-lend mode) with their rows flipping; (4) the corpus rewrite (one commit per tree: std, libs, examples); (5) docs. No workarounds; a compiler defect outside the unit is filed and reported. Report on this issue; the lead validates (adversarial probes on R4 at every call site, R6 on Data, R7 forwarding both directions, the seven opener entries) and submits the patch.\n\n## 5. Out of scope\n\nThe borrowing struct itself (`borrows struct`, `lends self`, the for-head window — design 275 U3, which this brief precedes). Mode-fixed exclusive lends (`-> &var T` that forbids shared narrowing) — no use case yet. `with_ref`/`with_var_ref` — untouched, they are the closure spellings and the lowering vocabulary. The generic statement-scoped borrow window (the follow-up U3 files). Retiring `#lend_var` — SL-335, after U3.\n","id":"SL-333","labels":["design","borrows","compiler"],"priority":"high","project":"SL","revision":11,"sequence":1121,"stage":"queued","status":"open","title":"borrows signatures name the lent reference and its mode: borrows -> &T / -> &var T, receiver bounds it, use site narrows, read-only lends exist (RULED Sep 20; brief in the description)","updated":"1790010449"},"before":{"assignee":"agent:claude-sl333","author":"agent:claude-sawlang","comments":[{"author":"agent:claude-sawlang","body":"Reply to c1 (lead, Sep 20): all three points folded; the description is revision 2. (1) R4 now separates the LENT VALUE's mode from the RECEIVER's required access as two facts: an accessor is SHARED-ELIGIBLE iff its shared specialization type-checks as an ordinary &self body under the existing receiver rule, EXCLUSIVE-ONLY otherwise, and the requirement is checked at EVERY call site — a read-only window through an exclusive-only accessor still borrows the receiver exclusively, is refused on a let root naming the mutating line, and two such windows on one var root conflict as two exclusive receiver borrows. The eligibility is recorded on the declaration as a declared annotation field (per instantiation for generics) so imported and generic accessors carry it; no syntactic writes-self scan. (2) R6 lifts #lend_var into &var self accessors and re-keys the design-179 two-specialization split on 'names #lend_var' rather than on the receiver spelling; eligibility is computed on the shared specialization AFTER folding #lend_var=false, so Data.[]'s CoW gate keeps let-reads shared and separation-free (K-f pins it). (3) R7: forwarded lends carry the OUTER window's mode — the shared specialization calls the inner accessor shared (a nested CoW read no longer copies), an outer &var T over an inner &T is refused at the lend, an exclusive-only inner propagates exclusive-only to the outer through the recorded eligibility (K-g pins all three). The forwarded lend is the seventh named entry of the window-opener funnel. Syntax and sequencing unchanged (after SL-318.p6 lands, before U3).\n","created":"1789949631","id":"c1"},{"author":"agent:claude-sl333","body":"BUILT (agent:claude-sl333, Sep 21): branch `sl333`, three commits, linear on `main` (ded0605a). Full battery GREEN, 31/31 stages. Awaiting the lead's validation + patch.\n\n- `7117b7d9` SL-333: `borrows` signatures name the lent reference and its mode (compiler R1-R7 + the std/libs/examples corpus + conformance rows K123-K130)\n- `f6fd70d8` SL-333: docs for the `borrows` mode rules (LANGUAGE_SPEC, saw-lang skill, README, todo.md)\n- `dbb61617` SL-333: the specialization twin is a sibling template, not a descendant (two terminal-battery findings, both fixed at the mechanism)\n\n## 1. ONE DIVERGENCE FROM THE RULED BRIEF — the lead should rule\n\nR6 says two things that cannot both hold, and I built the one that makes R7 true as written. **The split key is the DECLARED MODE, not the presence of `#lend_var`.**\n\nR6: \"make the specialization split key on 'the accessor names `#lend_var`' rather than on the receiver spelling\" and, one sentence later, \"An accessor that never names it compiles once, as before.\"\nR7: \"inside the SHARED specialization the inner accessor is invoked shared … inside the EXCLUSIVE one exclusively.\"\n\nA forwarding accessor that never names `#lend_var` has no shared specialization for R7 to speak about, so under the literal key R7 is unimplementable for it — and worse, R4 then makes EVERY such accessor exclusive-only, because its single `&var` lend borrows `self.inner` exclusively and that is a mutation through the receiver like any other. `Outer.[]` in `lend_var_coro_and_forwarding.saw` is exactly that shape and would have stopped working on a `let` root.\n\nWhat I built: a `borrows -> &var T` accessor compiles as two specializations (it is precisely the declaration a use site may open either way), a `borrows -> &T` one compiles once. `#lend_var` keeps its meaning unchanged — it NAMES the specialization being compiled — and the always-constant rule survives, re-keyed with the split: `#lend_var` in a `-> &T` accessor is always false and is the same error design 179 gave for the receiver spelling it used to key on (`errors/lend_var_in_shared_lend_accessor.saw`, the repurposed `lend_var_in_var_self_accessor.saw`).\n\nCost, measured: the shared copy of a `-> &var T` accessor is a second body checked and emitted. Nothing in the final binary for an accessor used one way only (unreferenced internal functions are DCE'd); the `bench` lane's checksums and timings are unchanged and the whole battery is green. If the lead prefers the literal key, R7's K129 cell 1 and the `lend_var_coro_and_forwarding` outcome flip back, and the report's OPEN moves there.\n\nTwo derived rules I wrote because the ruling's mechanism required them, both flagged:\n- `#lend_var` in a `borrows -> &T` accessor is an error (the design-179 rule re-keyed off the receiver onto the mode, same reason: an always-constant gate reads as a live decision).\n- R2 is checked after the receiver validation, so `&self ... borrows -> &var T` gets the mode error and a by-value `self` still gets the receiver error first.\n\n## 2. The window-opener funnel: seven entries × three outcomes\n\n`place_uses._window_call` is the one chokepoint; its docstring now names its entries as an author spells them, and both declaration facts (R1's mode, R4's eligibility) are read there and nowhere else. Every cell below has compile/run evidence — the permitted column from `examples/conformance/K123_every_window_opener_reads_the_declared_mode.saw` (runs, output asserted), the refused column from `examples/errors/shared_lend_write_refused_at_every_opener.saw` (six diagnostics in one compile) plus the two R7 error pins.\n\n| # | entry (author's spelling) | internal caller | `-> &T` + write | `-> &var T` shared | `-> &var T` exclusive |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | subscript read/chain `b[i]`, `b[i].n` | `_chain_window` | n/a (no write) | ok | — |\n| 2 | named accessor `b.at(i)`, `b.at(i).n` | `_chain_window` | n/a (no write) | ok | — |\n| 3 | write through either, `b[i] = x`, `b[i].n += 1` | `_assignment` | REFUSED (`v[…]`, `v.at(…)`) | — | ok |\n| 4 | `?.` chain assignment `b.pair(i)?.n = v` | `_chain_assign_window` | REFUSED (`v.pair(…)`) | — | ok |\n| 5 | `!` head, `b.get(i)!`, `b.pair(i)!.n = v` | `_chain_window` / `_assignment` | REFUSED (`v.pair(…)`) | ok | ok |\n| 6 | `match` / `if let` on a place (DF-146d payload lend) | `_borrow_match`, `_presence_condition` | n/a — the opener is shared by construction; the read compiles on both modes | ok | — |\n| 7 | reference argument `f(&b[i])` / `f(&var b[i])` | `_span_call` | REFUSED (`v[…]`) | ok | ok |\n| 8 | forwarded `lend self.inner[i]` inside another accessor | `_span_call` (from_lend) | REFUSED at the lend (`self.inner[…]`, R7 cell 2) | ok (R7 cell 1 — no inner gate) | ok |\n\n(Seven openers in the funnel, eight rows: the write and the `&var` argument are two spellings of one entry and two different things to a reader.)\n\nEach refusal names the accessor, its declared mode and the fixit, verbatim:\n``cannot write through `v[…]`: `View.[]` is declared `borrows -> &Int`, which lends the place READ-ONLY — a use site may narrow a declared window mode, never widen it`` / hint ``declare the accessor `borrows -> &var Int` to let a use site write through the place it lends (its receiver must then be `&var self`), or reach the storage through a `&var self` method of `View```.\n\n## 3. R4 eligibility per std accessor\n\nComputed by the receiver-permission funnel itself: `_self_borrow_is_exclusive`'s five named entry points now RECORD what they find as well as refuse it, onto `Method.place_receiver_mutation` (a DECLARED `annotation(...)` field — the `astgraft` lane is green), on the SHARED specialization, closed over the forwarding graph in `place_uses.propagate_forwarded_eligibility` before anything is lowered, and read at every call site by `_window_call` alone. The `lend`'s own reference is exempt by `from_lend` — that is the place the accessor exists to hand out.\n\n| accessor | declared | shared-eligible | why |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `Vector.[]` | `&var self -> &var T` | yes | body is a bounds check + `lend buf[index]` |\n| `Vector.get` | `&var self -> &var T?` | yes | same, conditional |\n| `Map.[]` | `&var self -> &var V?` | yes | `_find` is `&self`; the arm lends the payload |\n| `Map.get` | `&var self -> &var V?` | yes | same |\n| `Map._key_ref` | `&self -> &K?` | yes | `&self`, trivially |\n| `Map._value_ref` | `&self -> &V?` | yes | `&self`, trivially |\n| `Data.[]` | `&var self -> &var UInt8` | yes | the CoW gate (`self._make_ready`, a `&var self` call on `self`) folds OUT of the shared specialization — this is the R6 order the whole rule turns on |\n| `Box.value` | `&var self -> &var T` | yes | `lend self.ptr[0]` |\n| `JsonValue.as_array` | `&var self -> &var Vector<JsonValue>?` | yes | match arm payload lend |\n| `JsonValue.as_object` | `&var self -> &var Map<String, JsonValue>?` | yes | same |\n| `Slot.value` | `&var self -> &var T` | yes | a guard + `lend self.v!` |\n| `UnsafeRef.deref` | `&var self -> &var T` | yes | `lend self.p[0]` |\n| `TomlSection.table` | `&self -> &TomlTable?` | yes | `&self`, trivially |\n| `TomlDoc.section_at` | `&self -> &TomlSection` | yes | `&self`, trivially |\n| `TomlDoc.section` | `&self -> &TomlSection?` | yes | `&self`, trivially |\n\nEvery one of the fifteen is shared-eligible, so R2's move to `&var self` costs no caller a `let` root. Direct evidence: a probe that reads through `Vector.[]`, `Vector.get`, `Map.[]`, `Map.get`, `Data.[]`, `Box.value`, `JsonValue.as_object` and `Slot.value` on `let` bindings compiles and runs. The corpus's own exclusive-only examples are hand-written (`Bag.tally`, `Ledger.entry`, `Inner.[]` with an ungated gate).\n\n## 4. R6 — the `Data.[]` proof\n\n`examples/conformance/K128_cow_gate_survives_the_var_self_spelling.saw`, run output:\n\n```\nlet read 10, gates 0          # hand-written gate, `let` root: the gate does not run\nvar read 10, gates 0\nvar write 11, gates 1          # …and does on a write\nData: frozen read 7, no var needed\nData: after buf[0] = 90 -> frozen 7, buf 90     # the partner keeps its bytes\nData: two readers of one buffer see 7 and 7\n```\n\n`Data.[]` is now `&var self ... borrows -> &var UInt8` with the CoW gate under `#lend_var`. The shared specialization folds the constant false, the gate is REMOVED, R4 is computed on that body, and `let frozen = load(); print(frozen[0])` needs no `var` and separates nothing. `examples/data_place_shared_read.saw` and the `lend_var_*.saw` set keep their outcomes unchanged (K130's control row).\n\n## 5. R7 — three cells\n\n- **cell 1, shared forwarding costs nothing.** `K129_forwarded_lend_carries_the_window_mode.saw`: `shared forward reads 3, inner gates 0` / `exclusive forward writes 4, inner gates 1` / `shared forward reads again 4, inner gates 1`, and a nested `Data` read through a forwarding accessor leaves the sharing partner's bytes alone. `lend_var_coro_and_forwarding.saw` — design 179's own statement of the limit — CHANGED OUTCOME to `inner gates 0` / `inner gates 1` (was 1 / 3); its header now states R7 instead of the limit.\n- **cell 2, no upgrade.** `errors/forwarded_lend_cannot_upgrade_an_inner_shared_lend.saw`: an outer `-> &var Int` over an inner `-> &Int` is R1's refusal reaching entry 8 — ``cannot write through `self.inner[…]`: `Inner.[]` is declared `borrows -> &Int`…``, anchored at the `lend`.\n- **cell 3, exclusive-only propagates.** `errors/forwarded_lend_inherits_exclusive_only.saw`: ``cannot read through the place window `[]` opens on immutable variable `frozen`: … — the forwarded lend through `Inner.[]` at line 39``. Propagated through the RECORDED bit, as a fixpoint over the forwarding graph, so an imported or generic forwarder carries it.\n\n## 6. The corpus rewrite\n\n105 pre-existing declarations, all rewritten; 30 new ones in the K-row files.\n\n| tree | declarations | `-> &var T` | `-> &T` |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| `sawc/std` | 12 (6 files) | 10 | 2 (`Map._key_ref`, `Map._value_ref`) |\n| `libs/toml` | 3 (1 file) | 0 | 3 |\n| `examples` | 90 (61 files) | 57 | 33 |\n| (new K-row files) | 30 (12 files) | 19 | 9 + 2 deliberately malformed |\n| `blade`, `devtools`, `tools` | 0 | — | — |\n\nOracles, as the brief set them:\n- **std + libs by the API's intent.** Subscript / `get` / view-shaped: `Vector.[]`/`get`, `Map.[]`/`get`, `Data.[]`, `Box.value`, `Slot.value`, `UnsafeRef.deref`, `JsonValue.as_array`/`as_object` are `&var self -> &var T` (the spec's own worked examples write through every one, and `doc.as_object()!.insert(…)` in four corpus files is an exclusive use of `as_object`). Still not submitted as a patch.\n\n`aff3d4c4` SL-333: the receiver borrow's MODE is what the root charge compares\n\n## The mechanism\n\nIt WAS a special case, exactly as diagnosed: a bespoke scan (`place_uses._reject_nested_exclusive_receiver`) over the nested window lowering, which could only ever see TWO exclusive-only windows. Deleted.\n\nThe receiver borrow's mode is now ONE fact — `typechecker/types.py::_reference_access_mode`, \"the mode a by-reference argument borrows its ROOT at: the sigil, or exclusive when the accessor behind the place is exclusive-only\" — and it is the mode design 188's ordinary pairwise root-charge test reads when it builds its access entries, for top-level and nested reference arguments alike. Every pairing falls out of that one test; there is no second rule left.\n\nWHERE IT IS SETTLED, and why not at the call. A module type-checks its free functions BEFORE its extension methods, so a use site is routinely reached before the accessor whose BODY decides whether it requires an exclusive receiver — asking at the call would answer \"shared\" by accident of ordering. So a call whose access set still holds an unsettled place entry is kept, and those pairs are settled at END OF MODULE (`_settle_place_access_modes`), after every body has been checked. The forwarded-lend closure moved out of `place_uses` into the checker (`propagate_forwarded_place_eligibility`) and runs immediately before it, for the same reason and in that order. Only pairs BOTH sides of which read 'imm' at the call are looked at, so nothing is reported twice and no existing diagnostic moved.\n\n## The sibling matrix, compile evidence, before → after\n\nFixture: one `var` root, `Bag.[]` SHARED-ELIGIBLE, `Bag.tally` EXCLUSIVE-ONLY (epilogue write), `Bag.opt` exclusive-only and CONDITIONAL, `Wrap.fwd` exclusive-only by FORWARDING.\n\n| # | cell | before | after |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| a | `pair(&b[0], &b.tally(1))` — shared-eligible first | COMPILES, prints 30 | REFUSED |\n| b | `pair(&b.tally(0), &b[1])` — exclusive-only first | COMPILES, prints 30 | REFUSED |\n| c | `pair(&b.tally(0), &b.tally(1))` — both exclusive-only | refused (special case) | REFUSED (ordinary test) |\n| e | `pair_cell(&b[0], &b.opt(1)!)` — through a `!` head | COMPILES, prints 12 | REFUSED |\n| g | `with_root(&b.tally(0), &b)` — beside a plain `&root` | COMPILES, prints 10 | REFUSED |\n| h | `with_var_root(&b.tally(0), &var b)` — beside `&var root` | refused (sigil half) | REFUSED |\n| i | `with_var_root(&b[0], &var b)` — shared-eligible beside `&var root` | refused (sigil half) | REFUSED |\n| j | `pair(&w.fwd(0), &w.fwd2(1))` — both exclusive-only by FORWARDING | refused | REFUSED |\n| f | `pair(&b[0], &b[1])` — both shared-eligible (CONTROL) | compiles, 30 | COMPILES, 30 |\n\nThe lead's three probes, re-run on the tip: `p10_mixed_windows_conflict` and `p10c_exclusive_only_first` both refused, each naming `Bag.tally`, the line that mutates `self`, and saying the window only reads; `p10b_control_shared_beside_var` keeps design 188's own wording unchanged.\n\n## SL-334 — a PRE-EXISTING hole the sweep turned up, fixed in the same walk\n\nCell e was not R4's. `_build_access_path` — the walk that builds design 188's access entries — stepped through a `?.` hop and NOT through a `!`, so a `!`-headed place fell out to \"not a path\" and **never joined a call's access set at all**. QUEUED after design 275 U2 (SL-318.p6) lands and BEFORE U3 dispatches, because U3 writes the borrowing-struct spec text and its K rows against whatever spelling is current.\n\n## 0. Why\n\nTwo things, one earned by design 275 U3 and one older.\n(a) After U3, `borrows -> X` means two different things: design 141's \"lends a PLACE of X\" and U3's \"returns a VALUE of borrowing-struct type X that carries a borrow\". Same signature shape, disambiguated only by looking up whether X is declared `borrows struct` somewhere else — the reader-visibility doctrine's exact complaint. With `borrows -> &T` for a lend the signature says which: a `&T` result IS the place, a struct result is a value carrying one. And the rule unifies with the field rule U3 ruled: a reference type may appear in a RETURN position only under a `borrows` signature, and in a FIELD only under a `borrows struct` — one sentence, one check.\n(b) Design 141 made every lend writable at the use site's discretion (\"one `&self` declaration, the use site picks shared vs exclusive\"). An accessor AUTHOR therefore cannot express a read-only lend at all. The user's ruling reverses the default: the DECLARATION states the maximum mode, the use site narrows within it.\n\n## 1. The rules (RULED; R4/R6/R7 tightened per codex c1; R6 amended r3)\n\nR1. RETURN TYPE = the WINDOW's MAXIMUM mode. `borrows -> &var T` may open shared or exclusive windows; `borrows -> &T` opens shared only, and a write through it (`g.at(4).weight += 1`, `v[i] = x` through a `&T` subscript accessor, `&var g.at(4)` as an argument) is a compile error naming the accessor and its declared mode, with a fixit naming `-> &var T`. Conditional lends: `-> &var T?` / `-> &T?`. The bare `borrows -> T` spelling on a lend-shaped body is an ERROR with a fixit naming `-> &T` — no silent acceptance of both spellings (no-degradation doctrine). `borrows -> S` where `S` is a `borrows struct` stays the U3 value-returning form (this brief does not build it; it only leaves the spelling free).\nR2. RECEIVER MODE BOUNDS RETURN MODE. `-> &var T` REQUIRES `&var self`: an exclusive place cannot be projected from a shared receiver (design 106's no-upgrade rule one position over). `&var self borrows -> &T` is legal (an accessor that mutates its own bookkeeping but lends read-only). `&self borrows -> &var T` is the teaching error, fixit naming `&var self`.\nR3. THE USE SITE STILL NARROWS the WINDOW. A read through a `&var T` lend opens a SHARED window; two shared windows on one root compose; a write opens the exclusive one. Unchanged from design 141 for every use site within the declared maximum.\nR4. THE LENT VALUE's MODE and the RECEIVER's REQUIRED ACCESS are TWO facts (codex point 1). The window mode is R1/R3. The receiver access an accessor REQUIRES at a call is a property of its BODY: SHARED-ELIGIBLE if the shared specialization of the body (R6) type-checks as an ordinary `&self` body under the existing receiver rule (`typechecker/statements.py`'s \"a `&var self` method / a write reached through a `&self` receiver\" check — no mutation of `self` outside the lent place survives the fold); EXCLUSIVE-ONLY otherwise (a prologue counter, an epilogue update, a `self.x = …` beside the `lend`, a `&var self` method call on self — anything the fold does not remove). This is CHECKED AT EVERY CALL SITE, not only on `let` receivers: a shared use of a shared-eligible `&var self` accessor borrows the receiver SHARED (so it works on a `let` root and two such windows compose); a use of an exclusive-only accessor borrows the receiver EXCLUSIVELY even when the WINDOW it opens is read-only — so it is refused on a `let` root naming the mutating line, and TWO concurrent read-only windows through an exclusive-only accessor on one `var` root CONFLICT (two exclusive receiver borrows), exactly as two `&var self` calls would — and so does an exclusive-only window beside a shared-eligible one, or beside a plain `&`/`&var` of the root (lead validation c3: the receiver borrow's mode is the ONE fact the pairwise root-charge test compares, for every pair, never a special case for two exclusive-only windows). Two read-only results never imply two compatible receiver borrows. The eligibility is RECORDED on the declaration as a DECLARED annotation field (design 126 contract, `astgraft` lane), computed once from the specialized body, so IMPORTED and GENERIC accessors (per instantiation, since the fold's outcome can depend on the instantiation) carry it to their callers — never re-derived by a syntactic \"writes self\" scan at the use site. Ordinary (non-`borrows`) `&var self` methods are UNCHANGED: on a `let` receiver they stay an error.\nR5. ONE RULE FOR REFERENCE TYPES OUTSIDE PARAMETERS. `&T`/`&var T` is legal as a return type iff the function is `borrows` (it is the lend); as a struct field iff the struct is `borrows struct` (U3 builds that half; this brief adds the return half and writes the sentence once in the spec so U3 only adds its clause). Everywhere else a reference type in a non-parameter position keeps today's error.\nR6 (AMENDED r3). THE SPECIALIZATION SPLIT IS KEYED ON THE DECLARED MODE, and `#lend_var` IS LIFTED INTO `&var self` ACCESSORS (codex point 2). Today the constant is forbidden in a `&var self`-declared accessor because that spelling was exclusive-only at every use. Under R1-R4 the writable accessors — `Vector.[]`, `Data.[]` (the copy-on-write gate lives under `#lend_var`), every `&var self -> &var T` — move to that spelling and compile as TWO specializations through the EXISTING design-179 machinery. The key is the DECLARED MODE: every `borrows -> &var T` accessor compiles twice — it is precisely the declaration a use site may open either way, and R7 needs the shared body to EXIST even when the constant is never named (a forwarding accessor's inner call follows the outer window's mode; under r2's key \"names `#lend_var`\" a plain forwarder had no shared body, and R4 would have read its single `&var` lend of `self.inner` as a receiver mutation, making every forwarder exclusive-only) — and a `borrows -> &T` accessor compiles once. The shared specialization folds `#lend_var = false` and REMOVES what it gated, the exclusive one folds `true`. R4's eligibility is computed on the SHARED specialization AFTER the fold, so an exclusive-only CoW branch (`if #lend_var { self._make_ready(...) }`) does not make ordinary reads exclusive: `let frozen = load(); print(frozen[0])` keeps working with no separation and no `var`. The rule \"`#lend_var` is a compile error in a `&var self` accessor\" is DELETED from the spec and RE-KEYED onto the mode: naming `#lend_var` in a `borrows -> &T` accessor is the error (always false — one specialization, the constant decides nothing; design 179's always-constant rule, same reason); the rule \"outside a `borrows` body it is an error\" stays. NO new syntactic scan: eligibility = \"does the specialized shared body pass the ordinary permission rules\". The constant's RETIREMENT in favour of one declaration per lend mode is SL-335, sequenced after U3 — not this brief.\nR7. FORWARDED LENDS carry the OUTER window's mode (codex point 3). Today `lend other[i]` reaches the inner accessor EXCLUSIVELY whichever specialization runs, because `lend X` hands `X` over as `&var X` (spec: \"a shared read of a nested copy-on-write buffer will copy\"). Under R1-R3 the inner call is made with the OUTER window's mode: inside the SHARED specialization the inner accessor is invoked shared (its `&T` window, its shared specialization, no CoW separation), inside the EXCLUSIVE one exclusively. Two refusals fall out and are rows: an outer `-> &var T` accessor whose lend is backed by an inner `-> &T` accessor is an error at the outer `lend` (no upgrade — the inner cannot supply an exclusive place; fixit: declare the inner `-> &var T` or lend your own storage); an outer `-> &T` accessor forwarding through an inner `-> &var T` accessor never upgrades it (the inner is called shared, and the inner's R4 receiver requirement applies to the forwarded receiver: an exclusive-only inner accessor makes the outer exclusive-only too, propagated through the recorded eligibility). The spec sentence about the nested CoW copy is replaced by the new rule.\n\n## 2. The unit (one Opus agent; the corpus rewrite is mechanical and rides inside)\n\nCompiler: parser accepts a reference type after `borrows ->` (and `?`-suffixed); typechecker: (i) the lend-coverage check (design 141) also checks the lent place's mode against the declared return mode and the receiver mode (R1, R2); (ii) the use-site window opener reads the declared maximum and refuses a write through `&T` (R1) — ONE funnel (obligation 1): the place-window opener whose docstring names its entries (subscript `v[i]`, named accessor call, `?.` head, `!` head, `match`/`if let` payload lend per DF-146d, reference-argument `&v[i]`/`&var v[i]`, and the FORWARDED `lend X` inside another accessor — R7's entry); (iii) R4's receiver-eligibility bit, computed ONCE per accessor (per instantiation for generics) from the shared specialization via the existing receiver rule, recorded as a DECLARED annotation field, read at every call site by the same funnel to pick the receiver borrow's mode; (iv) R6: delete the `&var self` fence on `#lend_var`, make the specialization split key on the DECLARED RETURN MODE (r3) rather than on the receiver spelling; (v) R7: the forwarded-lend lowering passes the outer mode; (vi) the old bare spelling, the `&self -> &var T` combination, `#lend_var` in a `-> &T` accessor, and the R7 upgrade each get their fixit error. `--emit-docs` carries the return mode and the receiver eligibility.\nCorpus rewrite: 107 `borrows ->` declarations (Sep-20 census: sawc/std 6 files, examples 61, libs 1; blade/devtools/sawtracker none). Per-site oracle, no guessing: std + libs by the API's intent (a subscript / `get` / `with_*`-shaped accessor is `&var self -> &var T`; a view that must not be written through is `&self -> &T`; when the intent is not obvious from the docstring + existing uses, flag OPEN in the report rather than pick); examples from their use sites (any exclusive use anywhere → `&var`; otherwise `&`), each flip noted in the file's header comment where the file is a pin/row. Every existing test keeps its outcome unless the brief's rule changes it, and each changed outcome is listed. The `#lend_var` examples (`lend_var_*.saw`, `data_place_shared_read.saw`) are R6's regression set and must keep their outcomes; `lend_var_coro_and_forwarding.saw` is R7's, and its nested-CoW expectation changes if it pinned the copy.\nDocs (design 125): LANGUAGE_SPEC \"Places (`borrows` and `lend`)\" — the signature grammar, R1-R7, a new \"read-only lends\" paragraph, the `#lend_var` section's third rule deleted and the two-specializations paragraph re-keyed on the mode, the forwarded-lend sentence replaced, the conditional-lend spellings, the 14 mentions; saw-lang skill (7 mentions + the cheat-sheet line); README if it shows a `borrows` signature.\n\n## 3. Conformance rows FIRST (obligation 3 — a new guarantee: \"a shared lend cannot be written through\", and R4's \"receiver access is never widened by a read-only result\")\n\nK rows continue after U2's last number; the header count stays honest. Rows: (K-a) a write through a `-> &T` lend is refused at the use site, all seven entries of the window opener (matrix, one cell per entry incl. the forwarded lend); (K-b) `&self -> &var T` is refused at the declaration; (K-c) R4 both ways: a shared-eligible `&var self -> &var T` accessor used shared on a `let` receiver works and two such windows compose; an exclusive-only accessor (bookkeeping in the epilogue) is refused on a `let` receiver naming the line, AND two concurrent read-only windows through it on a `var` root are refused as two exclusive receiver borrows, AND (r3, c3) an exclusive-only window beside a shared-eligible one on the same root is refused in both orders; (K-d) the bare `borrows -> T` spelling is refused with the fixit; (K-e) `-> &var T?` / `-> &T?` conditional lends — present/absent × shared/exclusive; (K-f) R6: `Data.[]` declared `&var self -> &var UInt8` with its CoW gate under `#lend_var` — a read on a `let` binding does not separate and needs no `var`, a write separates, the sharing partner keeps its bytes; (K-g) R7: forwarding through the shared specialization calls the inner accessor shared (a nested CoW read does NOT copy — measured by the partner's bytes/refcount), an outer `&var T` over an inner `&T` is refused at the lend, an exclusive-only inner makes the outer exclusive-only; (K-h) control: existing window rows (LIFO epilogues, root charge, `v.push` inside a window) unchanged in outcome. Obligation 2 sweep (a behavioural-contract flip: use sites that WRITE through an accessor the rewrite classifies `&T` stop compiling; accessors whose body mutates self become exclusive-only at every call): the corpus rewrite IS the sweep — every site that stops compiling is a classification decision listed in the report, never a workaround.\n\n## 4. Gates + conduct\n\nPer-commit: full suite + freestanding (both arches) under the suite lock as CLAUDE.md prescribes; terminal: full battery incl. `irdet --all`, `reemit`, `bootstrap` (libs/blade compile under the new spelling), `corodiff`. Linear branch, commits: (1) K rows; (2) parser + typechecker + fixits (R1-R5), (3) R6 + R7 (the specialization re-key and the forwarded-lend mode) with their rows flipping; (4) the corpus rewrite (one commit per tree: std, libs, examples); (5) docs. No workarounds; a compiler defect outside the unit is filed and reported. Report on this issue; the lead validates (adversarial probes on R4 at every call site, R6 on Data, R7 forwarding both directions, the seven opener entries) and submits the patch.\n\n## 5. Out of scope\n\nThe borrowing struct itself (`borrows struct`, `lends self`, the for-head window — design 275 U3, which this brief precedes). Mode-fixed exclusive lends (`-> &var T` that forbids shared narrowing) — no use case yet. `with_ref`/`with_var_ref` — untouched, they are the closure spellings and the lowering vocabulary. The generic statement-scoped borrow window (the follow-up U3 files). The signature says what\\n+a caller may do with one — `borrows -> &var T` lends a place a use site may\\n+read or write, `borrows -> &T` lends a view — and the use site narrows within\\n+what the declaration allows:\\n+\\n+```saw-fragment\\n+extension Document {\\n+    // A read-only lend: callers reach the section where it sits, and nothing\\n+    // they write reaches this storage.\\n+    public func section(&self, name: String) borrows -> &Section? {\\n+        if let i = self._section_index(name) {\\n+            lend self.sections[i]\\n+        } else {\\n+            return None\\n+        }\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+```\\n \\n A method can also END its receiver. `consumes` says so at the declaration, and\\n `move` says so at the call:\\ndiff --git a/designs/todo.md b/designs/todo.md\\nindex 7dce881d..7d35e1a1 100644\\n--- a/designs/todo.md\\n+++ b/designs/todo.md\\n@@ -38,10 +38,12 @@ is scheduled and in what order is the whole of what they say.\\n - Design 259 — the self-hosted parser (designs/259-selfhost-parser.md; QUEUED Sep 1 by the user, §3 ruling batch fully RULED same day incl. Four units in order U4 → U1 → U2 → U3: the effect answer at the source, the frame LEDGER + `corodiscovery` gate lane, TOTALITY (no silent decline; SL-287, SL-316, K33–K37), then coverage SL-215 + SL-317 as epic units — NOT point fixes; sawtracker's build waits for the finished foundation (user ruling). A container that owns a block and has no row is an INVARIANT failure naming the AST class. TWO TOTALITY CHECKS beside it: (a) after the site census, a re-walk with an INDEPENDENT suspension predicate requires a recorded decision for every suspension position, and asks the table about every container it passes — so a NEW AST shape fails coverage until the table classifies it; (b) after every resume body is generated, a walk asserts that no `frame_boundary` callee survives as a plain call, naming the frame and the callee — `closure_calls_permitted`'s question made total and run for every frame, which is the runtime-facing half. `declined` is GONE from both ledger tables: a generic template is `template` (64 of U1's 67 rows — it owed no frame in the first place), a callee that owes one and has none is `refused`, and a site the transform will not lower is `refuse(<why>)`. THE FLIPS, each with its row: SL-287 (`FrameLedger.unbuildable_callee` is read by the ONE rejector, so a call to a frameless suspending callee is a compile error carrying the ledger's recorded reason); SL-316 (a closure body that really suspends is refused AT THE CLOSURE wherever written — the question is asked of the closure's own effect node, not of its caller, so the entry/imported divergence is gone); SL-326 + SL-330 (the generic promotion walked a positional guess and a `FunctionCall` shape; it walks every position and both call shapes now, so an ANF-hoisted generic call and a module-qualified one are promoted and embedded); SL-331 (consumption symmetry's two questions deadlocked across a TEMPLATE CHAIN — the candidate closure runs to a fixpoint before the `live` test now, which is the mechanism rather than the found symptom, probed: the issue's namespace hypothesis was wrong); SL-324 + SL-325 (three `might`-where-a-frame-is-meant reads flipped to `frame_boundary`, all three strictly widening). Conformance rows K118-K121; the `corototality` battery lane (`tools/test_coro_shapes.py`, six checks — three compare the table against `ast_walk`'s two enumerations and the transform's consumers, three INJECT the failure and require the named ICE, each beside an uninjected control). CORPUS COST, listed not absorbed: `examples/vector_closure_suspending_transform.saw` CHANGED OUTCOME to a refusal (design 216's real claim is covered by `vector_closures_over_nocopy_elements.saw`) and `examples/modules/sl306mod`'s three unused `each_yield_*` helpers moved to `sl316closure`, a fixture that exists to be refused. TWO USER RULINGS folded in Sep 20 before submission: **SL-323** — `existential_dispatch` MOVES OUT of `frame_boundary` into the conservative half beside `closure_call`, so the boundary is `cooperative | blocking | test_suspend` and a body whose only cause is a dispatch is not framed (design 223's refusal AT the dispatch is what keeps it sound — it fires before any framing question, so every dispatch that reaches one has all-sync implementations); `CONSERVATIVE_CAUSES` states the membership once, `conservative_only` joins the derivations beside `closure_only`, the K115 pin flips XPASS and its INDEX row is rewritten to the ruled answer, spec + skill updated. **SL-327** — REPRODUCED and fixed at the KEYING, no change to settling. The symptom is cache-state dependent, which is why it looked environmental: a FRESH std build agrees with itself, and five of eight cached blobs on this machine held a seed table keyed to an AST that was not the one stored beside them (`Command.output` keyed 20761 in the table and 62190 in both the AST and the namespace symbol), every one written by a process whose id bound was an order of magnitude past a fresh std build's — the state `_prepared_builtins`' own docstring says the pair must not be stored from. TWO keying fixes: `stdcache.store` refuses to publish an inconsistent pair, and `sawc._rekeyed_std_seed` re-keys a restored table onto the AST the compile actually holds (by `(owner, method)`, the granularity every other std census already uses), so blobs already on disk are harmless. Proven against the real bad blob: 18 dangling keys -> 0, and the namespace symbol's id seeded after and not before. `examples/errors/thread_body_reports_both_violations.saw` is the standing test, and it FLAPPED before the fix — passing on a freshly built cache and failing on a restored one, minutes apart in one tree. **Design 275 §3 RULING 2 built at the lead's validation:** the two call shapes design 44's by-value embedding cannot serve are rows in the shape table (`coro_shapes.PENDING_SL322`) and each refusal NAMES SL-322 — the K37 dispatch hint keeps DF-223b and adds the pending note, and the suspending-recursion message adds it too AND moved its anchor to the RECURSIVE FUNCTION's declaration (the note SL-280's landing asked for; it used to point at whichever root seeded the cycle walk, e.g. `func main`). Both messages render the note from the one row, both pins assert `SL-322`, the recursion pin pins the anchor line, and the `corototality` lane fails if the row, the raiser and the pin stop agreeing.
