Nineteen consumers routed (the census table is the ledger's docstring); `self._suspends` and the two `typechecker._*_suspending_methods_set` censuses are GONE, and `frame_keys.callee_frame_key` + `coro_ledger.method_frame_key` survive as the ledger's private composers — the transform no longer imports either. `--emit-frame-ledger` dumps both tables deterministically, and the `corodiscovery` battery lane (`tools/test_coro_discovery.py`, 9 checks — seven parse the module, two RUN the compiler) fails on any raw discovery read outside the builder. BEHAVIOUR-PRESERVING, measured: the dump is byte-identical across 2597 programs (examples + blade + libs + selfhost + devtools + the read-only sawtracker sources) before and after, and the design-220 optimized-IR artifacts match the pinned base (8d752a78) on all 1579 rows. CODEX r1 (SL-318.p4) requested changes on two gaps, both fixed in the branch's fifth commit: [P1] the miss invariant was not on the consumer path — the decision tables were filled only under `--emit-frame-ledger` and `free_call_frame` still turned a table miss into `None`, so a gap and a decided negative were one fact; the decision is THREE-VALUED now (`framed` / `no-frame-owed` / `declined(reason)`), recorded on every compile, and `free_call_frame` + `method_target`'s EMBED answer raise `LedgerMiss` naming the key, with the per-read absence-or-miss verdict written into every docstring and a lane check that DROPS one decision and requires the named-key ICE; [P2] driven METHOD ROOTS were missing from the SITE census (their FRAME row appeared beside `# sites: 0`), so discovery now resolves each root's declaration after the walk into a table of its own and the census walks it — nine corpus files gained `context=driven root` rows, six of them from zero, and the eleven bogus \\\"root whose AST no method table holds\\\" declines became real framed rows. U2's worklist, read off the dumps: 67 declined FRAME rows (49 generic METHOD templates, 15 generic FREE templates — both normal — and THREE the census found the walk never reached) and FOUR declined SITE rows, all in `examples/vector_closure_suspending_transform.saw` and all `context=closure body`: the effect graph gives `traverse` NO cause at all (its suspending calls sit inside `Vector.map`/`each`/`fold` closure literals, which raise the conservative cause on the METHOD and no direct edge on the caller), so the walk never reaches `slow_double`/`slow_show`/`slow_add`, no frame is built, and their `yield_now()` runs outside one — a PASSING corpus test whose own comment names the limitation. SL-316's shape, measured rather than inferred. NOT flipped, per the unit's contract: SL-323/K115's `existential_dispatch` membership, SL-324's `might`-where-a-frame-is-meant read, SL-287/SL-316's declines\\n   - **U4 LANDED Sep 19 (SL-318.p3, merge 668fb0c7).** The answer is a CAUSE SET per node, not the three values the brief drafted (lead amendment, Sep 18: a three-valued answer would have forced each consumer to re-derive its own subset privately, which is the secondary analysis the unit removes). ONE set-union pass over the effect graph (`effects.classify_suspensions`) computes which causes reach each node — `cooperative`, `blocking`, `test_suspend`, `closure_call`, plus `existential_dispatch`, a FIFTH cause the sweep found nobody had classified — and the four walkers it replaces (`suspends`, design 206's `really_suspending`, design 242's `suspends_ignoring_blocking`, SL-306's `suspends_ignoring_closure_calls`) are DELETED, each becoming one of five named derivations whose docstring lists its consumers: `might_suspend`, `wraps_main`, `refused_in_thread_body`, `frame_boundary`, `closure_only`. The std seed carries the SET — and, since the lead's review, a SOURCE PER CAUSE beside it — which fixed a cell nobody had measured (a `Thread.spawn` body was permitted to call a std method that parks cooperatively, because the seeded leaf answered off one representative label; the refusal then had nothing left to name and printed a `<suspension source>` placeholder). Conformance rows K111-K115 are the cause × context matrix. Findings recorded, not absorbed: SL-323, SL-324, SL-325, SL-326, SL-327\\n \\n+- SL-333 — `borrows` signatures name the lent reference and its mode (brief in the tracker issue; RULED Sep 20 by the user, r2 folds codex's review). QUEUED after design 275 U2 and BEFORE U3, which writes the borrowing-struct spec text against this spelling. **BUILT Sep 21 (branch `sl333`, awaiting the lead's validation + patch).** `borrows -> &T` / `-> &var T` (and the `?` twins): the DECLARATION states the maximum window mode, `&var self` is required to lend `&var` (R2), the use site narrows within it (R3), and a read-only lend exists at last — which is what `libs/toml`'s document views and `Map`'s key probe wanted and design 141 could not express. R4 separates the LENT VALUE's mode from the RECEIVER's required access: an accessor is SHARED-ELIGIBLE iff its shared specialization passes the ordinary receiver-permission rules, recorded by `_self_borrow_is_exclusive`'s own five entry points (which now RECORD as well as refuse) onto a declared annotation field and read at every call site by the one window funnel — so `let v` plus `v[0]` still compiles under R2, while an accessor that mutates `self` outside the place it lends is refused on a `let` root by name and two of its read-only windows on one `var` root conflict. R5 states the reference-in-a-return-position rule once, in the parser's return-clause funnel, leaving U3 only its field clause. R6 deletes the `&var self` fence on `#lend_var` and re-keys the two-specialization split from the receiver spelling onto the DECLARED MODE, with eligibility computed on the shared copy AFTER the fold — so `Data.[]`'s copy-on-write gate costs an ordinary read nothing. R7 gives a FORWARDED lend the enclosing specialization's mode, spending design 179's one stated v1 limit (a nested CoW read no longer copies) with two refusals falling out of the same fact. `place_uses._window_call` now names its seven author-visible entries and reads both declaration facts at every one; `--emit-docs` carries the mode and the eligibility. Corpus: 105 declarations (sawc/std 12, libs 3, examples 90), blade and libs/*/tests unchanged. Conformance rows K123-K130. Whether an unsupported closure is rejected cannot depend on an unrelated drive site, so the transform's ENTRY CONDITION gained its second half (`_has_suspending_closure_body`, `frame_boundary` over the closure nodes — the same derivation `closure_owns_suspension` reads, so the gate and the walk cannot disagree) and `_build_frame_ledger` answers a ROOTLESS ledger instead of `None`: the body tables and the cause set, no frames, no sites, which is all a question about a BODY needs. The bit is computed once, from the specialized body, and stored as a DECLARED annotation field (design 126), so imported and generic accessors carry it to their callers and no \\\"writes self\\\" scan runs at a use site. 4], slots: [Slot; 2], pairs: [Cell; 2] }\\n+\\n+extension Bag {\\n+    // The WRITABLE declaration: a use site may open either flavor.\\n+    public func [](&var self, i: Int) borrows -> &var Int {\\n+        if i < 0 || i >= 4 { panic(\\\"Bag.[]: index out of range\\\") }\\n+        lend self.cells[i]\\n+    }\\n+\\n+    public func at(&var self, i: Int) borrows -> &var Int {\\n+        if i < 0 || i >= 4 { panic(\\\"Bag.at: index out of range\\\") }\\n+        lend self.cells[i]\\n+    }\\n+\\n+    public func get(&var self, i: Int) borrows -> &var Int? {\\n+        if i < 0 || i >= 4 { return None }\\n+        lend self.cells[i]\\n+    }\\n+\\n+    public func slot(&var self, i: Int) borrows -> &var Slot {\\n+        if i < 0 || i >= 2 { panic(\\\"Bag.slot: index out of range\\\") }\\n+        lend self.slots[i]\\n+    }\\n+\\n+    public func pair(&var self, i: Int) borrows -> &var Cell? {\\n+        if i < 0 || i >= 2 { return None }\\n+        lend self.pairs[i]\\n+    }\\n+\\n+    // Entry 8: a FORWARDED lend. It carries THIS specialization's mode, so the\\n+    // shared copy reaches `[]` shared and the exclusive one reaches it\\n+    // exclusively (R7).\\n+    public func forwarded(&var self, i: Int) borrows -> &var Int {\\n+        lend self.cells[i]\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+// The READ-ONLY declaration: every use site opens a shared window, and nothing\\n+// may widen it.\\n+struct View { cells: slot(&self, i: Int) borrows -> &Slot {\\n+        if i < 0 || i >= 2 { panic(\\\"View.slot: index out of range\\\") }\\n+        lend self.slots[i]\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+func bump(n: &var Int) { n = n + 1 }\\n+\\n+func main() {\\n+    var b = Bag(cells: [10, 20, 30, 40],\\n+                slots: [Slot.Filled(n: 1), Slot.Empty],\\n+                pairs: [Cell(n: 5), Cell(n: 6)])\\n+\\n+    print(\\\"1 subscript read {b[0]}\\\")\\n+    print(\\\"2 named read {b.at(1)}\\\")\\n+\\n+    b[0] += 1\\n+    print(\\\"3 write {b[0]}\\\")\\n+\\n+    b.pair(1)?.n = 21\\n+    print(\\\"4 chain write {b.pair(1)!.n}\\\")\\n+\\n+    print(\\\"5 forced read {b.get(0)!}\\\")\\n+\\n+    match b.slot(0) {\\n+        case Filled(_) -> { print(\\\"6 match sees Filled\\\") },\\n+        case Empty -> { print(\\\"6 match sees Empty\\\") }\\n+    }\\n+\\n+    bump(&var b[0])\\n+    print(\\\"7 ref arg {b.forwarded(0)}\\\")\\n+    print(\\\"8 forwarded {b.forwarded(0)}\\\")\\n+\\n+    // A `let` root may still READ through a writable accessor: the body mutates\\n+    // nothing outside the place it lends, so it is shared-eligible (R4).\\n+    let v = View(cells: [10, 20, 30, 40],\\n+ -> \\\"Empty\\\"\\n+    }\\n+    print(\\\"read-only: subscript {v[0]}, named {v.at(0)}, forced {v.get(0)!}, match {matched}\\\")\\n+    print(\\\"-- done\\\")\\n+}\\ndiff --git /dev/null\\n+++ b/examples/conformance/K125_receiver_access_is_a_second_fact.saw\\n@@ -0,0 +1,61 @@\\n+// Conformance row K125 — the LENT VALUE's mode and the RECEIVER's required\\n+// access are TWO facts (SL-333 R4), permitted half\\n+//\\n+// The window's mode comes from the declaration and the use site (R1/R3). The\\n+// receiver access an accessor REQUIRES at a call is a property of its BODY: it\\n+// is SHARED-ELIGIBLE when its shared specialization passes the ordinary\\n+// receiver-permission rules — nothing writes `self`, calls a `&var self` method\\n+// on it, or re-borrows it `&var`, outside the place it lends — and\\n+// EXCLUSIVE-ONLY otherwise.\\n+//\\n+// This file pins the shared-eligible side, which is what makes R2's `&var self`\\n+// requirement livable: a writable accessor is still READABLE on a `let` root,\\n+// and two such reads compose, because a read borrows the receiver SHARED out of\\n+// the same `&var self` declaration.\\n+//\\n+// The refused side is `errors/exclusive_only_accessor_on_let_root.saw` (a `let`\\n+// root, naming the mutating line) and\\n+// `errors/two_exclusive_only_windows_conflict.saw` (two read-only windows on one\\n+// `var` root, refused as two exclusive receiver borrows).\\n+// EXPECT: success\\n+// EXPECT-OUTPUT:\\n+// let root reads 10\\n+// two shared windows compose 30\\n+// var root writes 11\\n+// exclusive-only on a var root 20, visits 1\\n+// -- done\\n+\\n+struct Bag { cells: [Int; 4], visits: Int }\\n+\\n+extension Bag {\\n+    // SHARED-ELIGIBLE: the body lends and does nothing else.\\n+    public func [](&var self, i: Int) borrows -> &var Int {\\n+        if i < 0 || i >= 4 { panic(\\\"Bag.[]: index out of range\\\") }\\n+        lend self.cells[i]\\n+    }\\n+\\n+    // EXCLUSIVE-ONLY: the epilogue mutates the receiver, so every use site —\\n+    // this read included — borrows it exclusively.\\n+    public func tally(&var self, i: Int) borrows -> &var Int {\\n+        if i < 0 || i >= 4 { panic(\\\"Bag.tally: index out of range\\\") }\\n+        lend self.cells[i]\\n+        self.visits = self.visits + 1\\n+    }\\n+\\n+    public func visits_seen(&self) -> Int { self.visits }\\n+}\\n+\\n+func pair(a: &Int, b: &Int) -> Int { a + b }\\n+\\n+func main() {\\n+    let frozen = Bag(cells: [10, 20, 30, 40], visits: 0)\\n+    print(\\\"let root reads {frozen[0]}\\\")\\n+    print(\\\"two shared windows compose {pair(&frozen[0], &frozen[1])}\\\")\\n+\\n+    var b = Bag(cells: [10, 20, 30, 40], visits: 0)\\n+    b[0] += 1\\n+    print(\\\"var root writes {b[0]}\\\")\\n+\\n+    print(\\\"exclusive-only on a var root {b.tally(1)}, visits {b.visits_seen()}\\\")\\n+    print(\\\"-- done\\\")\\n+}\\ndiff --git a/examples/conformance/K127_conditional_lend_modes.saw b/examples/conformance/K127_conditional_lend_modes.saw\\nnew file mode 100644\\nindex 00000000..0a763b45\\n--- /dev/null\\n+++ b/examples/conformance/K127_conditional_lend_modes.saw\\n@@ -0,0 +1,78 @@\\n+// Conformance row K127 — the conditional lend carries a mode too:\\n+// `borrows -> &var T?` and `borrows -> &T?` (SL-333 R1)\\n+//\\n+// `?` rides the LENT TYPE and is the lend's own presence, exactly as it was for\\n+// the bare spelling — so the four spellings are `-> &T`, `-> &var T`, `-> &T?`\\n+// and `-> &var T?`, and the optional one adds nothing to the mode rule. The\\n+// matrix this file covers is present/absent × shared/exclusive, for both modes:\\n+//\\n+//                       present, shared   present, exclusive   absent\\n+//   `-> &var Cell?`     read                 write             None / no window\\n+//   `-> &Cell?`         read                 REFUSED (see\\n+//                                            `shared_lend_write_refused_at_every_opener.saw`)\\n+//\\n+// The absent path opens no window and runs no epilogue, so it is mode-free by\\n+// construction; it is here because \\\"no window\\\" must keep meaning \\\"no window\\\"\\n+// under a rule that is about window modes.\\n+// EXPECT: success\\n+// EXPECT-OUTPUT:\\n+// writable present, shared 5\\n+// writable present, exclusive 6\\n+// writable absent: none, epilogues 0\\n+// readable present, shared 5\\n+// readable absent: none\\n+// presence test on a writable lend: yes\\n+// presence test on a readable lend: yes\\n+// epilogues after four present lends: 4\\n+// -- done\\n+\\n+struct Cell { n: Int }\\n+\\n+struct Bag { rows: [Cell; 2], closes: Int }\\n+\\n+extension Bag {\\n+    // The WRITABLE conditional lend. The epilogue counts window CLOSES, which\\n+    // is how the absent path proves it ran none.\\n+    public func slot(&var self, i: Int) borrows -> &var Cell? {\\n+        if i < 0 || i >= 2 { return None }\\n+        lend self.rows[i]\\n+        self.closes = self.closes + 1\\n+    }\\n+\\n+    public func closes_seen(&self) -> Int { self.closes }\\n+}\\n+\\n+struct View { rows: [Cell; 2] }\\n+\\n+extension View {\\n+    // The READ-ONLY conditional lend.\\n+    public func slot(&self, i: Int) borrows -> &Cell? {\\n+        if i < 0 || i >= 2 { return None }\\n+        lend self.rows[i]\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+func main() {\\n+    var b = Bag(rows: [Cell(n: 5), Cell(n: 9)], closes: 0)\\n+    print(\\\"writable present, shared {b.slot(0)!.n}\\\")\\n+\\n+    b.slot(0)!.n = 6\\n+    print(\\\"writable present, exclusive {b.slot(0)!.n}\\\")\\n+\\n+    var absent = Bag(rows: [Cell(n: 5), Cell(n: 9)], closes: 0)\\n+    let missing = if let _ = absent.slot(7) { \\\"some\\\" } else { \\\"none\\\" }\\n+    print(\\\"writable absent: {missing}, epilogues {absent.closes_seen()}\\\")\\n+\\n+    let v = View(rows: [Cell(n: 5), Cell(n: 9)])\\n+    print(\\\"readable present, shared {v.slot(0)!.n}\\\")\\n+    let vmissing = if let _ = v.slot(7) { \\\"some\\\" } else { \\\"none\\\" }\\n+    print(\\\"readable absent: {vmissing}\\\")\\n+\\n+    let present = if let _ = b.slot(1) { \\\"yes\\\" } else { \\\"no\\\" }\\n+    print(\\\"presence test on a writable lend: {present}\\\")\\n+    let vpresent = if let _ = v.slot(1) { \\\"yes\\\" } else { \\\"no\\\" }\\n+    print(\\\"presence test on a readable lend: {vpresent}\\\")\\n+\\n+    print(\\\"epilogues after four present lends: {b.closes_seen()}\\\")\\n+    print(\\\"-- done\\\")\\n+}\\ndiff --git a/examples/conformance/K128_cow_gate_survives_the_var_self_spelling.saw b/examples/conformance/K128_cow_gate_survives_the_var_self_spelling.saw\\nnew file mode 100644\\nindex 00000000..7b6fb721\\n--- /dev/null\\n+++ b/examples/conformance/K128_cow_gate_survives_the_var_self_spelling.saw\\n@@ -0,0 +1,70 @@\\n+// Conformance row K128 — `#lend_var` lives in a `&var self ... -> &var T`\\n+// accessor, and its copy-on-write gate costs a READ nothing (SL-333 R6)\\n+//\\n+// R2 moves every writable accessor to `&var self`, which is the spelling design\\n+// 179 forbade `#lend_var` in. R6 lifts that fence and re-keys the split onto the\\n+// DECLARED MODE, which is what now says whether a use site can open both\\n+// flavors: `-> &var T` compiles as two specializations, the authored\\n+// declaration folding the constant FALSE and the twin folding it TRUE.\\n+//\\n+// R4 is then computed on the SHARED specialization AFTER the fold, which is the\\n+// whole load-bearing claim: the gate is a `&var self` call on `self`, so an\\n+// accessor that kept it would be EXCLUSIVE-ONLY and `let frozen = load()` plus\\n+// `frozen[0]` would stop compiling. It is folded out, so the shared body mutates\\n+// nothing, the read borrows the receiver shared, and `std.data`'s own subscript\\n+// — the language's worked example of this — keeps working on a `let` binding\\n+// with no separation.\\n+//\\n+// Both halves are here: a hand-written accessor with a visible gate counter, and\\n+// `Data` itself, where the observable is the SHARING PARTNER's bytes.\\n+// EXPECT: success\\n+// EXPECT-OUTPUT:\\n+// let read 10, gates 0\\n+// var read 10, gates 0\\n+// var write 11, gates 1\\n+// Data: frozen read 7, no var needed\\n+// Data: after buf[0] = 90 -> frozen 7, buf 90\\n+// Data: two readers of one buffer see 7 and 7\\n+// -- done\\n+import std.data.*\\n+\\n+struct Bag { cells: [Int; 4], gates: Int }\\n+\\n+extension Bag {\\n+    func separate(&var self) { self.gates = self.gates + 1 }\\n+\\n+    public func [](&var self, i: Int) borrows -> &var Int {\\n+        if i < 0 || i >= 4 { panic(\\\"Bag.[]: index out of range\\\") }\\n+        if #lend_var {\\n+            self.separate()\\n+        }\\n+        lend self.cells[i]\\n+    }\\n+\\n+    public func gates_seen(&self) -> Int { self.gates }\\n+}\\n+\\n+func main() {\\n+    let frozen = Bag(cells: [10, 20, 30, 40], gates: 0)\\n+    print(\\\"let read {frozen[0]}, gates {frozen.gates_seen()}\\\")\\n+\\n+    var b = Bag(cells: [10, 20, 30, 40], gates: 0)\\n+    print(\\\"var read {b[0]}, gates {b.gates_seen()}\\\")\\n+    b[0] += 1\\n+    print(\\\"var write {b[0]}, gates {b.gates_seen()}\\\")\\n+\\n+    var built = try! Data(capacity: 2)\\n+    try! built.push(7u8)\\n+    try! built.push(8u8)\\n+\\n+    let frozen_data = built\\n+    print(\\\"Data: frozen read {frozen_data[0]}, no var needed\\\")\\n+\\n+    var buf = frozen_data\\n+    buf[0] = 90u8\\n+    print(\\\"Data: after buf[0] = 90 -> frozen {frozen_data[0]}, buf {buf[0]}\\\")\\n+\\n+    let second = frozen_data\\n+    print(\\\"Data: two readers of one buffer see {frozen_data[0]} and {second[0]}\\\")\\n+    print(\\\"-- done\\\")\\n+}\\ndiff --git a/examples/conformance/K129_forwarded_lend_carries_the_window_mode.saw b/examples/conformance/K129_forwarded_lend_carries_the_window_mode.saw\\nnew file mode 100644\\nindex 00000000..e4a5ce18\\n--- /dev/null\\n+++ b/examples/conformance/K129_forwarded_lend_carries_the_window_mode.saw\\n@@ -0,0 +1,82 @@\\n+// Conformance row K129 — a FORWARDED lend reaches the inner accessor at the\\n+// OUTER window's mode (SL-333 R7), cell 1 of 3\\n+//\\n+// `lend self.inner[i]` hands another accessor's place on. Design 179 lowered\\n+// `lend X` to `__window(&var X)` unconditionally, so the inner accessor was\\n+// always reached EXCLUSIVELY — sound (separating storage is never wrong) but a\\n+// shared read of a nested copy-on-write buffer copied for nothing, and design\\n+// 179 shipped that as a stated v1 limit.\\n+//\\n+// The forwarded lend is the seventh entry of the window-opener funnel, and R7\\n+// gives it the same answer as the other six: the mode comes from the window\\n+// being opened. The outer accessor's SHARED specialization lends `&X`, so the\\n+// inner call is a shared window — no gate, no separation; its EXCLUSIVE twin\\n+// lends `&var X` and reaches the inner exclusive specialization.\\n+//\\n+// The other two cells are refusals:\\n+//   `errors/forwarded_lend_cannot_upgrade_an_inner_shared_lend.saw` — an outer\\n+//     `-> &var T` backed by an inner `-> &T` is refused AT THE LEND;\\n+//   `errors/forwarded_lend_inherits_exclusive_only.saw` — an EXCLUSIVE-ONLY\\n+//     inner accessor makes the outer one exclusive-only too, propagated through\\n+//     the recorded eligibility rather than re-derived.\\n+// EXPECT: success\\n+// EXPECT-OUTPUT:\\n+// shared forward reads 3, inner gates 0\\n+// exclusive forward writes 4, inner gates 1\\n+// shared forward reads again 4, inner gates 1\\n+// nested CoW: outer read 7, partner still 7\\n+// -- done\\n+import std.data.*\\n+\\n+struct Inner { cells: [Int; 2], gates: Int }\\n+\\n+extension Inner {\\n+    func run_gate(&var self) { self.gates = self.gates + 1 }\\n+\\n+    public func [](&var self, i: Int) borrows -> &var Int {\\n+        if i < 0 || i >= 2 { panic(\\\"Inner.[]: index out of range\\\") }\\n+        if #lend_var { self.run_gate() }\\n+        lend self.cells[i]\\n+    }\\n+\\n+    public func gates_seen(&self) -> Int { self.gates }\\n+}\\n+\\n+struct Outer { inner: Inner }\\n+\\n+extension Outer {\\n+    public func [](&var self, i: Int) borrows -> &var Int {\\n+        lend self.inner[i]\\n+    }\\n+\\n+    public func inner_gates(&self) -> Int { self.inner.gates_seen() }\\n+}\\n+\\n+// The nested copy-on-write case the v1 limit was really about: a `Data` reached\\n+// through a forwarding accessor. A shared read must not separate, so the\\n+// partner sharing the buffer keeps its bytes AND its identity.\\n+struct Blob { bytes: Data }\\n+\\n+@synthesize extension Blob: Copy {}\\n+\\n+extension Blob {\\n+    public func [](&var self, i: Int) borrows -> &var UInt8 {\\n+        lend self.bytes[i]\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+func main() {\\n+    var o = Outer(inner: Inner(cells: [3, 4], gates: 0))\\n+    print(\\\"shared forward reads {o[0]}, inner gates {o.inner_gates()}\\\")\\n+    o[0] += 1\\n+    print(\\\"exclusive forward writes {o[0]}, inner gates {o.inner_gates()}\\\")\\n+    print(\\\"shared forward reads again {o[0]}, inner gates {o.inner_gates()}\\\")\\n+\\n+    var built = try! Data(capacity: 2)\\n+    try! built.push(7u8)\\n+    try! built.push(8u8)\\n+    let partner = built\\n+    let blob = Blob(bytes: partner)\\n+    print(\\\"nested CoW: outer read {blob[0]}, partner still {partner[0]}\\\")\\n+    print(\\\"-- done\\\")\\n+}\\ndiff --git a/examples/conformance/M22_place_write_let_root.saw b/examples/conformance/M22_place_write_let_root.saw\\nindex A\\n-// `borrows` accessor spelled `&self` is borrowed with the WINDOW's flavor,\\n-// chosen at each use site -- shared for a read, exclusive for a write -- so\\n-// calling it shared-only in generated docs would say the opposite of the truth.\\n+// SL-333 puts BOTH facts a caller needs into the entry. `returns` carries the\\n+// declared window mode -- `&var Int` may be written through, `&Int` may only be\\n+// read -- and `\\\"self\\\"` carries R4's receiver eligibility: `\\\"window\\\"` for an\\n+// accessor whose receiver is borrowed with the window's own flavor (so a `let`\\n+// root may read through it), `\\\"borrows-var\\\"` for an EXCLUSIVE-ONLY one, whose\\n+// body mutates `self` outside the place it lends and which therefore takes the\\n+// receiver exclusively at every use site, reads included. the fixit names the receiver, because the receiver is\\n+// what has to change. The accessor's body\\n+// mutates `self` outside the place it lends — the epilogue counts the visit —\\n+// so every call takes the whole receiver exclusively, exactly as a `&var self`\\n+// method does. What the caller sees is not \\\"you may not write this\\\", which\\n+// would be about the window; it is \\\"this accessor cannot be used on an\\n+// immutable binding at all\\\", which is about the body, so the body's line is in\\n+// the message.\\n+// EXPECT: error\\n+// EXPECT-ERROR-CONTAINS: cannot read through the place window `tally` opens on immutable variable `frozen`\\n+// EXPECT-ERROR-CONTAINS: the accessor borrows its receiver exclusively at every use site, because its body mutates `self`\\n+// EXPECT-ERROR-CONTAINS: a write into receiver storage at line 24\\n+\\n+struct Bag { cells: [Int; 4], visits: Int }\\n+\\n+extension Bag {\\n+    public func tally(&var self, i: Int) borrows -> &var Int {\\n+        if i < 0 || i >= 4 { panic(\\\"Bag.tally: index out of range\\\") }\\n+        lend self.cells[i]\\n+        self.visits = self.visits + 1\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+func main() {\\n+    let frozen = Bag(cells: [10, 20, 30, 40], visits: 0)\\n+    print(\\\"{frozen.tally(0)}\\\")\\n+}\\ndiff --git a/examples/errors/forwarded_lend_cannot_upgrade_an_inner_shared_lend.saw b/examples/errors/forwarded_lend_cannot_upgrade_an_inner_shared_lend.saw\\nnew file mode 100644\\nindex 00000000..c35b84af\\n--- /dev/null\\n+++ b/examples/errors/forwarded_lend_cannot_upgrade_an_inner_shared_lend.saw\\n@@ -0,0 +1,36 @@\\n+// SL-333 R7 (conformance row K129, cell 2 of 3): an outer `borrows -> &var T`\\n+// whose lend is backed by an inner `borrows -> &T` is refused AT THE LEND.\\n+//\\n+// No upgrade: the inner accessor lends read-only, so it cannot supply the\\n+// exclusive place the outer declaration promises its callers. The forwarded\\n+// lend is the seventh entry of the window-opener funnel, so this is R1's\\n+// refusal reaching it — the exclusive specialization's `lend` is a write\\n+// through a read-only lend, and it is named as one.\\n+//\\n+// The two ways forward are the declaration's: widen the inner accessor, or lend\\n+// storage the outer type owns itself.\\n+// EXPECT: error\\n+// EXPECT-ERROR-CONTAINS: cannot write through `self.inner[…]`: `Inner.[]` is declared `borrows -> &Int`, which lends the place READ-ONLY\\n+// EXPECT-ERROR-CONTAINS: declare the accessor `borrows -> &var Int`\\n+\\n+struct Inner { cells: [Int; 2] }\\n+\\n+extension Inner {\\n+    public func [](&self, i: Int) borrows -> &Int {\\n+        if i < 0 || i >= 2 { panic(\\\"Inner.[]: index out of range\\\") }\\n+        lend self.cells[i]\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+struct Outer { inner: Inner }\\n+\\n+extension Outer {\\n+    public func [](&var self, i: Int) borrows -> &var Int {\\n+        lend self.inner[i]\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+func main() {\\n+    var o = Outer(inner: Inner(cells: [3, 4]))\\n+    o[0] = 9\\n+}\\ndiff --git a/examples/errors/forwarded_lend_inherits_exclusive_only.saw b/examples/errors/forwarded_lend_inherits_exclusive_only.saw\\nnew file mode 100644\\nindex 00000000..2c1c09e0\\n--- /dev/null\\n+++ b/examples/errors/forwarded_lend_inherits_exclusive_only.saw\\n@@ -0,0 +1,46 @@\\n+// SL-333 R7 (conformance row K129, cell 3 of 3): an EXCLUSIVE-ONLY inner\\n+// accessor makes the FORWARDING accessor exclusive-only too.\\n+//\\n+// R4's eligibility is closed over the forwarding graph before anything is\\n+// lowered, and the reason is the ordinary receiver rule rather than a new one:\\n+// the forwarded lend opens a window on `self.inner`, which is storage inside\\n+// this accessor's own receiver, and that window borrows `self.inner`\\n+// EXCLUSIVELY because the inner accessor's body mutates its own receiver. A\\n+// mutation through the receiver is a mutation through the receiver, whichever\\n+// construct spells it — so the outer accessor takes the whole of `self`\\n+// exclusively at every use site, reads included, and is refused on a `let` root\\n+// naming the forward.\\n+//\\n+// The propagation is through the RECORDED bit, never re-derived at the use site:\\n+// that is what lets an IMPORTED or generic forwarding accessor carry the fact to\\n+// its callers.\\n+// EXPECT: error\\n+// EXPECT-ERROR-CONTAINS: cannot read through the place window `[]` opens on immutable variable `frozen`\\n+// EXPECT-ERROR-CONTAINS: the forwarded lend through `Inner.[]`\\n+\\n+struct Inner { cells: [Int; 2], gates: Int }\\n+\\n+extension Inner {\\n+    func run_gate(&var self) { self.gates = self.gates + 1 }\\n+\\n+    // EXCLUSIVE-ONLY: the prologue mutates the receiver on BOTH\\n+    // specializations, so nothing folds it away.\\n+    public func [](&var self, i: Int) borrows -> &var Int {\\n+        if i < 0 || i >= 2 { panic(\\\"Inner.[]: index out of range\\\") }\\n+        self.run_gate()\\n+        lend self.cells[i]\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+struct Outer { inner: Inner }\\n+\\n+extension Outer {\\n+    public func [](&var self, i: Int) borrows -> &var Int {\\n+        lend self.inner[i]\\n+    }\\n+}\\n+\\n+func main() {\\n+    let frozen = Outer(inner: Inner(cells: MODE.\\n+//\\n+// Design 179 refused `#lend_var` in a `&var self` accessor, because that\\n+// spelling was then exclusive-only at every use: the constant would always be\\n+// true while reading as a live decision. R1 moved that fact into the return\\n+// type, so the declaration that opens one flavor only is now `borrows -> &T` —\\n+// a read-only lend, where the constant is always FALSE. Same rule, same reason,\\n+// the key it was always about. It does.\\n //\\n-// The pessimization: `lend other[i]` forwards another accessor's place, and\\n-// `lend X` lowers to a `&var X` argument, so the inner accessor is reached\\n-// exclusively no matter which specialization of the OUTER one is running. Sound — separating\\n-// storage is never wrong, only wasteful — but a shared read of a nested\\n-// copy-on-write buffer would copy. So a shared read through\\n+// `Outer` reaches `Inner` SHARED and runs no gate at all, and only the write\\n+// does. Design 179 shipped this as a known v1 limit (`lend X` was always\\n+// `&var X`, so three gate runs served one read, one write and one more read);\\n+// the gate counts below are what R7 costs instead.\\n // EXPECT: success\\n // EXPECT-OUTPUT:\\n // task results 12 22\\n-// forwarded read 3, inner gates 1\\n-// forwarded write 4, inner gates 3\\n+// forwarded read 3, inner gates 0\\n+//       return getattr(decl, 'place_receiver_mutation', None)\\n+\\n+    def _settle_place_access_modes(self) -> None:\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"Re-run the pairwise root-charge test over the calls whose This is the same overlap test with the same charge; what\\n+        changed is one entry's MODE.\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+        pending = self._deferred_place_access\\n+ root,\\n+                                       line, column) -> None:\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"Design 188's overlap diagnostic for the pair R4 decides (SL-333).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+        self._error(\\n+ If the inner accessor is EXCLUSIVE-ONLY, that window borrows\\n+        `self.inner` exclusively — a mutation through this accessor's receiver\\n+        like any other — so the property propagates. It is the same receiver\\n+        rule the checker applied to every other construct in the body, applied\\n+        to the one construct it deliberately does not judge: the window call\\n+        the place lowering synthesizes.\\n+\\n+        The direction matters. The exclusive\\n+        twin forwards exclusively, but its own eligibility is never asked: only\\n+        an exclusive use site reaches it.\\n+\\n+        Run per module at the end of its check, so every accessor it can reach\\n+        has been checked — a module's dependencies are checked before it, and\\n+        its own forwarding chains settle in the fixpoint below.\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+        edges = []\\n+        self._collect_place_forward_edges(program, edges)\\n+        if not edges:\\n+            return\\n+        changed = True\\n+        while changed:\\n+            changed = False\\n+            for decl, struct, method, exclusive, line in edges:\\n+                if getattr(decl, 'place_receiver_mutation', None) is not None:\\n+                    continue\\n+                if not exclusive:\\n+                    info = self.namespace.lookup_method(struct, method)\\n+                    inner = (getattr(info, 'ast_node', None)\\n+                             if info is not None else None)\\n+                    if getattr(inner, 'place_receiver_mutation', None) is None:\\n+                        continue\\n+                decl.place_receiver_mutation = (\\n+                    f\\\"the forwarded lend through `{struct}.{method}` at \\\"\\n+                    f\\\"line {line}\\\")\\n+                changed = True\\n+\\n+    def _collect_place_forward_edges(self, program, edges) -> None:\\n+        for ext in (getattr(program, 'extensions', None) or []):\\n+            for decl in (getattr(ext, 'methods', None) or []):\\n+                if getattr(decl, 'place_type', None) is None:\\n+                    continue\\n+                if getattr(decl, 'place_var_twin', False):\\n+                    continue      # only an exclusive use site reaches the twin\\n+                stack = [getattr(decl, 'body', None)]\\n+                seen = set()\\n+                while stack:\\n+                    node = stack.pop()\\n+                    if node is None or id(node) in seen:\\n+                        continue\\n+                    seen.add(id(node))\\n+                    stack.extend(child_nodes(node))\\n+                    if not (isinstance(node, ReferenceExpr)\\n+                            and getattr(node, 'from_lend', False)):\\n+                        continue\\n+                    inner = node.expr\\n+                    if self._place_use_receiver(inner) is None:\\n+                        continue\\n+                    if self._access_path_root(\\n+                            self._place_use_receiver(inner)) != \\\"self\\\":\\n+                        # about `self`.\\n+                        continue\\n+                    edges.append((decl, inner.place_struct, inner.place_method,\\n+                                  bool(node.mutable), node.line))\\n+        for decl in (getattr(program, 'module_decls', None) or []):\\n+            body = getattr(decl, 'body', None)\\n+            if body is not None:\\n+                self._collect_place_forward_edges(body, edges)\\n+\\n+    def _access_path_root(self, expr):\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"The NAME an access path is rooted at, or None.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+        node = expr\\n+        guard = 0\\n+        while node is not None and guard < 64:\\n+            guard += 1\\n+            if isinstance(node, Identifier):\\n+                return node.name\\n+            if isinstance(node, SelfExpr):\\n+                return \\\"self\\\"\\n+            receiver = self._place_use_receiver(node)\\n+            node = (receiver if receiver is not None\\n+                    else self._access_path_parent(node))\\n+        return None\\n+\\n     def _render_move(self, expr: Expression) -> str:\\n         if isinstance(expr, MoveExpr):\\n             return expr.variable\\n\",\"id\":\"SL-333\",\"request_id\":\"pid-74339-1790010448-7000\",\"title\":\"SL-333: borrows signatures name the lent reference and its mode — borrows -> &T / -> &var T (+ ? twins), receiver bounds return mode, use site narrows, R4 receiver eligibility recorded per declaration and fed to the root charge (mixed cell fixed at the mechanism), R6 split keyed on the DECLARED MODE (amended r3, user-ruled), R7 forwarded lends carry the outer mode; 105 declarations rewritten (std 12 / libs 3 / examples 90), K123-K130 + X41; fixes SL-334 (a !-headed place never joined the root charge); battery 31/31\"}","request_id":"pid-74339-1790010448-7000","sequence":1121,"timestamp":"1790010449"}
